<!-- example-1: body_md -->
\label{ex:gillespie-resource}
	Fix a colony of $N$ individuals with a total amount of available resource equal to $R:=(1-\kappa)N\ge1$. Suppose there are two competing types. Type $A$ individuals require only $1-\kappa$ units of resource to reach reproductive age, while type $a$ individuals require one unit, where $\kappa\in[0,1)$. If the current frequency of type $A$ is $x\in[0,1]$, then the  number of individuals that can reach reproductive age is
	\begin{align*}
		N_R(x):=\max\left\{m\in\mathbb{N}:\; m x(1-\kappa)+m(1-x)\le R\right\}=\left\lfloor\frac{R}{1-\kappa x}\right\rfloor\le N.
	\end{align*}
	Thus the effective population size is no longer constant, but depends on the current type frequency.

	These $N_R(x)$ individuals are chosen uniformly at random among the $N$ individuals. Let $\xi^R_g$ be the type-$A$ frequency at generation $g$ and $W^R_g$ the type-$A$ frequency among its $N_R(\xi^R_g)$ chosen individuals. Each of the $N$ individuals of generation $g+1$ independently has type $A$ with probability $p_R(W^R_g)$, so
	\begin{align*}
		\xi^R_{g+1}=\frac{1}{N}\mathrm{Bin}\bigl(N,p_R(W^R_g)\bigr),
		\qquad
		p_R(x):=\frac{x}{x+(1+\beta_R)(1-x)},
	\end{align*}
	where $\beta_R\ge0$ is the selective advantage of type $a$.
	The key feature is that, uniformly in $x$ and for bounded $\beta_R$, the one-step variance is $x(1-x)/N_R(x)+O(\beta_R/R)$ (exactly $x(1-x)/N_R(x)$ when $\beta_R=0$), so it depends on the frequency through $N_R(x)$, while the mean change is $p_R(x)-x+O(\beta_R/R)$, whose leading term does not. In particular, taking $\beta_R=1/R$ and speeding up time by $R$, one obtains as $R\to\infty$ the diffusion (by a standard diffusion approximation, see e.g.~\cite{ethier2009markov}*{Chapter~7, Theorem~4.1})
	\begin{align*}
		dX_t=-X_t(1-X_t)\,dt+\sqrt{X_t(1-X_t)(1-\kappa X_t)}\,dB_t,
	\end{align*}
	whose noise coefficient is no longer the classical Wright--Fisher term $X_t(1-X_t)$ but carries the factor $1-\kappa X_t$. This is the Wright--Fisher diffusion with efficiency of~\cite{casanova2020wright}*{(1.2)}. It is also the limit of the model of~\cite{casanova2020wright} in which an offspring that would exceed the resource is rejected~\cite{alsmeyer2026two}*{Theorem~2.2}.
<!-- end example-1 -->
<!-- example-2: body_md -->
Imagine  that there is a frequency-dependent population size, skewed offspring distribution and classic selection $s_N(x)=s$ for some $s\in[0,1)$.

	In order to construct $\mathcal W$, we let
	\begin{align*}
		\hat w_i(x) = Y_iB_i(x),
	\end{align*}
	where $\{Y_i\}_{i \in [N]}$ are i.i.d.\ $\mathrm{Gamma}(a,1)$ random variables for some $a>0$, and $B_i(x) \sim \mathrm{Bernoulli}\bigl(\frac{1-\kappa}{1-\kappa x}\bigr)$, i.i.d.\ and independent of $\{Y_i\}_{i\in[N]}$, for some $\kappa \in (0,1)$. For $\sum_{i \in [N]} \hat w_i(x) >0$, we set
	\begin{align*}
		w_i(x):=  \frac{\hat w_i(x)}{\sum_{j \in [N]} \hat w_j(x)}.
	\end{align*}
	Else, set $w_i(x) = \frac{1}{N}$ for all $i \in [N]$. Since the $Y_i$ are i.i.d.\ $\mathrm{Gamma}(a,1)$, given $J(x):=\{i:B_i(x)=1\}\neq\emptyset$ the weights $(w_i(x))_{i\in J(x)}$ are $\mathrm{Dirichlet}(a,\dots,a)$ distributed, and $w_i(x)=0$ for $i\notin J(x)$.
<!-- end example-2 -->
<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label{def: update operator}
	For each $x\in[N^{-1}]$, let $\mathcal W_x^N=(w_1^x,\dots,w_N^x)$ be an independent random vector with law $\mathcal W^N(x)$. Define
	\[
		W_A^N(x):=\sum_{i\in I_x} w_i^x.
	\]
	Conditioned on $\mathcal W_x^N$, let
	\[
		K_x^N\sim \mathrm{Binomial}\bigl(N,
		\widehat P_N(x,\mathcal W_x^N)\bigr).
	\]
	Assume moreover that the family $\{K_x^N\}_{x\in [N^{-1}]}$ is conditionally independent given $\{\mathcal W_x^N\}_{x\in [N^{-1}]}$. We then define the random map $\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N:[N^{-1}]\to [N^{-1}]$ by
	\[
		\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(x):=\frac{K_x^N}{N},\qquad x\in [N^{-1}].
	\]
	Since $\mathcal W^N(x)$ is exchangeable, the law of $\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(x)$ does not depend on the particular choice of the deterministic set $I_x$ with cardinality $Nx$. If $\{\widetilde{\mathcal R}_g^N\}_{g\ge 1}$ are i.i.d. copies of $\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N$, then the one-colony frequency process is given by
	\[
		X_g^N=\widetilde{\mathcal R}_g^N(X_{g-1}^N),\qquad g\ge 1.
	\]
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- thm-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:canning_convergence}
	Let $b,\sigma$ in~\eqref{eq:bsigma def} be continuous. Suppose for each $u_0 \in \mathcal C(\mathbb{R};[0,1])$, there exists a (probabilistically) unique weak solution to
	\begin{align}\label{eq:spde_canning}
		\partial_t u = \frac{\alpha}{2}\Delta u + B(u) + \sqrt{\sigma(u)u(1-u)}\dot{W},\qquad u(0,\cdot) = u_0.
	\end{align}
	Suppose for some $p>8$ and $\varepsilon >0$, uniformly in $N$ and $x\in[N^{-1}]$,
	\begin{align}\label{ineq:p_moment_canning_thm}
		\mathbb{E}_x \left[\abs{W_A^N(x) - x}^p \right] \lesssim h_N^{4+\varepsilon}.
	\end{align}
	Assume in addition that $\widetilde\xi_0^N\to u_0$ in $\mathcal C^\mathrm{tem}_\mathbb{R}$ as $N\to\infty$. Then the spatially interpolated process $\widetilde \xi^N$ as in~\eqref{eq:xin} converges in (probabilistic) distribution to the solution of~\eqref{eq:spde_canning} in  $\mathcal D(\mathbb{R}_+;\mathcal C^\mathrm{tem}_\mathbb{R})$.
<!-- end thm-1 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
\label{rem:existence}
	Note that the proof of Theorem \ref{thm:canning_convergence}, along with examples in Section \ref{sec:examples} gives a constructive proof to the existence of solution for equation \eqref{eq:spde_canning} when $\min\sigma>0$, which is an alternative method to Shiga~\cite{shiga1994two}. In addition, we do not assume continuity of the prelimit coefficients, $s_N,\, c_N$ as in \eqref{eq:bsigma def}.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
Note that in this work, we do require a priori that the equation~\eqref{eq:spde_canning} have weak uniqueness.
	Corollary~\ref{cor:AT conditions} below uses the mutation terms in~\eqref{eq: full CG drift} to construct discrete approximations for the full finite-invariant-interval class covered by the Athreya--Tribe uniqueness result.
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
Under~\eqref{ineq:p_moment_canning_thm}, reproduction events in which one parent produces a positive fraction of its colony are too rare to appear in the limit. Therefore the limit has continuous sample paths. In non-spatial models, such large reproduction events lead to $\Lambda$-coalescents~\cites{pitman1999coalescents,sagitov1999general} and to jumps in the frequency process~\cite{mohle2001forward}.
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- thm-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:main}
	Let $b$ and $\sigma$ in~\eqref{eq:coefficient convergence} be continuous with
	\begin{align*}
		b(0) \ge 0,\quad b(1) \le 0,\qquad \sigma(0) = \sigma(1) = 0.
	\end{align*}
	Suppose for all $u_0\in \mathcal C(\mathbb{R};[0,1])$, the equation~\eqref{eq: spde target} has a unique weak solution with initial condition $u(0,\cdot)=u_0$. If $\widetilde \xi_0^L \to u_0$ in $\mathcal C_\mathbb{R}^\text{tem}$, then $\widetilde{\xi}^L$ converges in distribution in $\mathcal D(\mathbb{R}_+;\mathcal C_\mathbb{R}^\text{tem})$ to the solution of~\eqref{eq: spde target}.
<!-- end thm-2 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lem:wf-floor}
	For every $w\in\mathcal S_N$ one has $\sum_{i=1}^N w_i^2\ge 1/N$, with equality precisely for the uniform vector $w_i\equiv 1/N$. Consequently $c_N(x)\ge 1/N$ for every colony model, so under the normalization $h_N=1/N$ the diffusion profile satisfies $\sigma(x)\ge 1$, the value $\sigma\equiv 1$ corresponding to Wright--Fisher resampling.
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
By the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality, $1=\Parentheses*{\sum_{i}w_i}^2\le N\sum_{i}w_i^2$, with equality if and only if all $w_i$ are equal. Taking expectations in~\eqref{eq:cn scaling} gives $c_N(x)\ge 1/N=h_N$, and hence $\sigma(x)=\lim_{N \to \infty}c_N(x)/h_N\ge 1$.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
\label{def:pop-update}
	For $N\ge N_0$ and $x\in[0,1]$, define the  effective population size
	\begin{align}\label{eq:pop-target-size}
		r_N(x)
		&:={\frac1{h_N\sigma(x)}}
		={\frac{N\sigma_*}{\sigma(x)}}.
	\end{align}
	For each $x \in [0,1]$, let $M_N(x)$ be a random variable taking values in $\{\lfloor r_N(x)\rfloor,\,\lfloor r_N(x)\rfloor+1\}$ with probabilities
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:pop-size-rounding}
		\begin{gathered}
			\mathbb{P}\Parentheses*{M_N(x)=\lfloor r_N(x)\rfloor}
			={\frac{\lfloor r_N(x)\rfloor
			\Parentheses*{\lfloor r_N(x)\rfloor+1-r_N(x)}}{r_N(x)}},\\
			\mathbb{P}\Parentheses*{M_N(x)=\lfloor r_N(x)\rfloor+1}
			={\frac{\Parentheses*{\lfloor r_N(x)\rfloor+1}
			\Parentheses*{r_N(x)-\lfloor r_N(x)\rfloor}}{r_N(x)}}.
		\end{gathered}
	\end{equation}
	Conditional on $M_N(x)$, choose independently a uniform subset $J_N(x)\subset[N]$ of cardinality $M_N(x)$ and set
	\begin{align}\label{eq:pop-active-weights}
		w_i^N(x)
		&:={\frac{\1_{\{i\in J_N(x)\}}}{M_N(x)}},
		\qquad i\in[N].
	\end{align}
	Define the selection functions and mutation rates  by
	\begin{align}\label{eq:pop-selection-mutation}
		s_N(x):= h_N^2 b(x),\text{ for } x \in [0,1],
		\qquad
		\theta_N^\pm:= h_N^2\theta^\pm.
	\end{align}
	By~\eqref{ineq:pop-admissible-range}, the selection and mutation parameters satisfy the admissibility conditions in Section~\ref{sec:canning}. Together with the weights above, they define a paintbox CG model and its update operator $\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N$ through Definition~\ref{def: update operator}.
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
\label{rem:pop-floor}
    In principle, we can set $M_N(x)= \lfloor r_N(x)\rfloor$ to be deterministic and it will not affect our results. Indeed, in this case, $c_N(x)=1/\lfloor r_N(x)\rfloor$ satisfies $0\le c_N(x)/h_N-\sigma(x)\le\|\sigma\|_\infty/(r_N(x)-1)$ instead of~\eqref{eq:pop-exact-coalescence}, so~\eqref{eq:bsigma def} still holds, and $M_N\ge r_N/2$, so Proposition~\ref{prop:pop-moment-limits} and Corollary~\ref{cor:pop-limit} hold with the same proofs. However the above choices simplify many calculations.
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- prop-1: body_md -->
\label{prop:pop-calibration}
	For every $N\ge N_0$ and $x\in[0,1]$, the random weight vector in Definition~\ref{def:pop-update} is exchangeable and takes values in $\mathcal S_N$. Moreover, almost surely,
	\begin{align}\label{ineq:pop-size-calibration}
		\left|
		\frac{M_N(x)}N-\frac{\sigma_*}{\sigma(x)}
		\right|
		&\le\frac1N,
		&
		\frac{N\sigma_*}{2\norm{\sigma}_\infty}
		&\le M_N(x)\le N,
	\end{align}
	and the coalescence functional satisfies the exact identity
	\begin{align}\label{eq:pop-exact-coalescence}
		c_N(x)
		&:={\mathbb{E}\Bracket*{\sum_{i=1}^N{w_i^N(x)}^2}}
		={\mathbb{E}\Bracket*{\frac1{M_N(x)}}}
		={h_N\sigma(x)}.
	\end{align}
<!-- end prop-1 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
By~\eqref{eq:pop-scale},~\eqref{ineq:pop-admissible-range}, and~\eqref{eq:pop-target-size},
	\begin{align*}
		2
		&\le\frac{N\sigma_*}{\norm{\sigma}_\infty}
		\le r_N(x)
		\le N.
	\end{align*}
	We see by definition that $\abs{M_N(x)-r_N(x)}\le1$ and $M_N(x)\le N$ almost surely. Hence
	\begin{align*}
		\left|
		\frac{M_N(x)}N-\frac{\sigma_*}{\sigma(x)}
		\right|
		&{\overset{\eqref{eq:pop-target-size}}{=}}
		\frac1N\abs{M_N(x)-r_N(x)}
		\le\frac1N.
	\end{align*}
	Moreover,
	\begin{align*}
		M_N(x)
		&\ge\lfloor r_N(x)\rfloor
		\ge r_N(x)-1
		\ge\frac{r_N(x)}2
		\ge\frac{N\sigma_*}{2\norm{\sigma}_\infty},
	\end{align*}
	which proves~\eqref{ineq:pop-size-calibration}.

	Conditional on $M_N(x)$, the set $J_N(x)$ is uniform among subsets of $[N]$ with the prescribed cardinality. Hence the weights are exchangeable, nonnegative, and sum to one. Moreover,
	\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i = 1}^N {w_i^N(x)}^2 \right]
		{\overset{\eqref{eq:pop-active-weights}}{=}}\mathbb{E}\Bracket*{\frac1{M_N(x)}}
		{\overset{\eqref{eq:pop-size-rounding}}{=}}\frac{1}{r_N(x)}
		{\overset{\eqref{eq:pop-target-size}}{=}}h_N\sigma(x).
	\end{align*}
	This proves~\eqref{eq:pop-exact-coalescence}.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- prop-2: body_md -->
\label{prop:pop-moment-limits}
	For every fixed $p\ge2$, there exists $C>0$ so that for all $N\ge N_0$,
	\begin{align}\label{ineq:pop-type-moment}
		\sup_{x\in[N^{-1}]}
		\mathbb{E}_x\Bracket*{\abs{W_A^N(x)-x}^p}
		&\le Ch_N^{p/2}.
	\end{align}
<!-- end prop-2 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
Fix $x\in[N^{-1}]$.  Conditional on $M_N(x)=m$, one has
	\begin{align*}
		mW_A^N(x)
		={\abs{J_N(x)\cap I_x}}
		\sim\operatorname{Hypergeometric}\Parentheses*{N,Nx,m}.
	\end{align*}
	In particular, $\mathbb{E}_x[W_A^N(x)\mid M_N(x)]=x$.  The Hoeffding--Serfling inequality for sampling without replacement~\cite{serfling1974probability} gives, for every $t>0$,
	\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{P}_x\Parentheses*{\abs{W_A^N(x)-x}\ge t}
		&{=}\mathbb{E}_x\Bracket*{
		\mathbb{P}_x\Parentheses*{\abs{W_A^N(x)-x}\ge t\mid M_N(x)}}\\
		&\le2\mathbb{E}_x\Bracket*{\exp\Parentheses*{-2M_N(x)t^2}}\\
		&{\overset{\eqref{ineq:pop-size-calibration}}{\le}}
		2\exp\Parentheses*{-\frac{t^2}{h_N\norm{\sigma}_\infty}}.
	\end{align*}
	Consequently,
	\begin{align*}
		\sup_{x\in[N^{-1}]}
		\mathbb{E}_x\Bracket*{\abs{W_A^N(x)-x}^p}
		&{=}\sup_{x\in[N^{-1}]}
		p\int_0^\infty t^{p-1}
		\mathbb{P}_x\Parentheses*{\abs{W_A^N(x)-x}\ge t}\,\mathrm dt\\
		&\le2p\int_0^\infty
		t^{p-1}\exp\Parentheses*{-\frac{t^2}{h_N\norm{\sigma}_\infty}}\,\mathrm dt\\
		&{=}Ch_N^{p/2},
	\end{align*}
	for some $C>0$ independent of $N$, which is~\eqref{ineq:pop-type-moment}.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- cor-1: body_md -->
\label{cor:pop-limit}
	Fix $\alpha\in(0,1)$. Suppose that, for every $u_0\in\mathcal C(\mathbb{R};[0,1])$, the equation
	\begin{align}\label{eq:pop-limit-spde}
		\partial_tu
		&{=}\frac\alpha2\Delta u+b(u)u(1-u)
		+\theta^+(1-u)-\theta^-u
		+\sqrt{\sigma(u)u(1-u)}\,\dot W,
		\qquad u(0,\cdot)=u_0,
	\end{align}
	has a $[0,1]$-valued weak solution that is unique in law.  Construct the spatial CG model from Definition~\ref{def:pop-update}, with scalar normalization $h_N$ as in~\eqref{eq:pop-scale}. If $\widetilde\xi_0^N\to u_0$ in $\mathcal C_\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{tem}}$, then $\widetilde\xi^N$ converges in distribution to the solution of~\eqref{eq:pop-limit-spde} in $\mathcal D(\mathbb{R}_+;\mathcal C_\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{tem}})$.

	In particular, for $b\equiv-1$ and $\sigma(x)=1-\kappa x$, where $0\le\kappa<1$, one has
	\begin{align*}
		\sigma_*
		&{=}1-\kappa,
		&
		h_N
		&{=}\frac1{N(1-\kappa)},
		&
		r_N(x)
		&{=}\frac{N(1-\kappa)}{1-\kappa x}.
	\end{align*}
	The resulting spatial CG models converge to the two-way-mutation Gillespie--FKPP equation
	\begin{align}\label{eq:gillespie-fkpp-mutation}
		\partial_tu
		{=}\frac\alpha2\Delta u-u(1-u)
		+\theta^+(1-u)-\theta^-u
		+\sqrt{u(1-u)(1-\kappa u)}\,\dot W.
	\end{align}
<!-- end cor-1 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
Note that Proposition~\ref{prop:pop-calibration} verifies~\eqref{eq:bsigma def} exactly. Fix $p>8$ and choose $0<\varepsilon<p/2-4$.  Since $h_N\le1$, we see by Proposition~\ref{prop:pop-moment-limits} that there exists $C>0$ so that for all $N\ge N_0$,
	\begin{align*}
		\sup_{x\in[N^{-1}]}
		\mathbb{E}_x\Bracket*{\abs{W_A^N(x)-x}^p}
		&{\overset{\eqref{ineq:pop-type-moment}}{\le}}C h_N^{p/2}
		\le C h_N^{4+\varepsilon}.
	\end{align*}
	Thus~\eqref{ineq:p_moment_canning_thm} holds, and Theorem~\ref{thm:canning_convergence} gives the asserted convergence. The specialization follows by substituting $b\equiv-1$ and $\sigma(x)=1-\kappa x$ into~\eqref{eq:pop-limit-spde}.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
\label{rem:resource-example}
	For $N\ge N_0$, Example~\ref{ex:gillespie-resource} with selection under the diffusive scaling $\beta_R:=h_N^2/(1-h_N^2)$ is the CG model of Definition~\ref{def:pop-update} with
	\begin{align*}
		\sigma(x)=\varepsilon^2(1-\kappa x),\qquad b\equiv-1,\qquad \theta^\pm=0,\qquad M_N(x)=\lfloor r_N(x)\rfloor,
	\end{align*}
	the last choice being allowed by Remark~\ref{rem:pop-floor}. Indeed, $\sigma_*=\varepsilon^2(1-\kappa)$, so $h_N=1/(\varepsilon^2R)$ and $r_N(x)=R/(1-\kappa x)$, hence $M_N(x)=N_R(x)$: the active parents are the individuals that reach reproductive age. Since $c_N(x)=1/N_R(x)$, for $\kappa>0$ the noise becomes smaller as type $A$ individuals become more common. Here, Corollary~\ref{cor:pop-limit} with Remark~\ref{rem:pop-floor} shows that the spatial version of Example~\ref{ex:gillespie-resource} converges to~\eqref{eq:gillespie-fkpp}. For every $u_0\in\mathcal C(\mathbb{R};[0,1])$, this equation has a $[0,1]$-valued weak solution by Remark~\ref{rem:existence}, and it is unique in law by moment duality, see~\cite{shiga1988stepping} and~\cite{barnes2025wright}*{Theorem~1.1} for $\kappa=0$, and~\cite{hou2026subcritical}*{Proposition~3.3 and Lemma~3.4} for $0<\kappa<1$.
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- prop-3: body_md -->
\label{prop:dir-calibration}
	For every $N\ge N_0$ and $x\in[0,1]$,
	\begin{align}\label{eq:dir-exact-calibration}
		c_N(x)=\mathbb{E}\Bracket*{\sum_{i=1}^N{w_i^N(x)}^2}=h_N\sigma(x).
	\end{align}
<!-- end prop-3 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
By~\eqref{eq:dirichlet-concentration}, it holds that
	\begin{align}\label{eq:prop:dir-calibration step 1}
		a_N(x)+1= \frac{(N-1)h_N\sigma(x)}{Nh_N\sigma(x) - 1},\qquad Na_N(x)+1
		=
		\frac{N-1}{Nh_N\sigma(x)-1}.
	\end{align}
	For a symmetric $\operatorname{Dirichlet}(a,\ldots,a)$ vector, the moment formula for Dirichlet distribution (cf.~\cite{ng2011dirichlet}*{Eq.~(2.5), p.~39}) gives
	\[
		\mathbb{E}\Bracket*{{w_i^N(x)}^2}
		=
		\frac{a_N(x)(a_N(x)+1)}{Na_N(x)(Na_N(x)+1)}
		=
		\frac{a_N(x)+1}{N(Na_N(x)+1)},\qquad i = 1,\dots, N.
	\]
	Therefore, by symmetry,
	\[
		\mathbb{E}\Bracket*{\sum_{i=1}^N{w_i^N(x)}^2}
		= N\mathbb{E}\Bracket*{w_1^N(x)^2}=
		\frac{a_N(x)+1}{Na_N(x)+1}
		\overset{\eqref{eq:prop:dir-calibration step 1}}{=}
		h_N\sigma(x),
	\]
	which implies~\eqref{eq:dir-exact-calibration}.

	If $h_N\sigma(x) = 1/N$, the weights are deterministically uniform, so~\eqref{eq:dir-exact-calibration} holds trivially.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- prop-4: body_md -->
\label{prop:dir-moment-limits}
	Let $\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N$ be the update operator of Definition~\ref{def: update operator} associated with the weights above and the selection functions in~\eqref{eq:dir-selection}. Suppose also that, for some constants $\theta^+,\theta^-\ge0$,
	\begin{align}\label{eq:dir-mutation-scaling}
		\lim_{N\to\infty}h_N^{-2}\theta_N^\pm
		&{=}\theta^\pm.
	\end{align}
	Then one has
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:dir-coefficient-limits}
		\begin{aligned}
			\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{x\in[N^{-1}]}
			\Bigg(
			&\abs{
			h_N^{-2}\mathbb{E}_x\Bracket*{\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(x)-x}- b(x)x(1-x)-\theta^+(1-x)+\theta^-x
			}\\
			&\quad
			+\abs{
			h_N^{-1}\mathbb{E}_x
			\Bracket*{\Parentheses*{
			\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(x)-x
			}^2
			}
			-\sigma(x)x(1-x)
			}
			\Bigg)
			=
			0.
		\end{aligned}
	\end{equation}
	Moreover, for every fixed $p\ge2$, there are constants $C>0$ and $N_p\ge N_0$ such that, for every $N\ge N_p$, the following bounds hold:
	\begin{align}
		\sup_{x\in[N^{-1}]}
		\mathbb{E}_x\Bracket*{
		\abs{W_A^N(x)-x}^p
		}
		&\le
		Ch_N^{p/2},
		\label{eq:dir-type-moment}\\
		\sup_{x\in[N^{-1}]}
		\mathbb{E}_x\Bracket*{
		\abs{\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(x)-x}^p
		}
		&\le
		Ch_N^{p/2}.
		\label{eq:dir-update-moment}
	\end{align}
<!-- end prop-4 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
It suffices to consider $N\ge N_0$.  Fix $p\ge2$.  For $x\in[N^{-1}]$, if $x\in\{0,1\}$ or $a_N(x)=\infty$, then $W_A^N(x)=x$ deterministically.  At every remaining grid point, the gamma representation of a Dirichlet vector \cite{ng2011dirichlet}*{Thm.~2.1, Eq.~(2.7), p.~40} and additivity of independent gamma variables give
	\[
		W_A^N(x)
		\sim
		\operatorname{Beta}\Parentheses*{
		Nxa_N(x),N(1-x)a_N(x)
		}.
	\]
	The standard beta mean and variance formulas (cf.~\cite{ng2011dirichlet}*{Eq.~(2.6), p.~39}), together with the deterministic cases, give
	\begin{align}\label{eq:dir-W-first-two-moments}
		\mathbb{E}_x\Bracket*{W_A^N(x)}
		=
		x,\qquad
		\text{var}_x\Parentheses*{W_A^N(x)}
		=
		\frac{x(1-x)}{Na_N(x)+1}
		\overset{\eqref{eq:dir-total-concentration}}{=}
		x(1-x)\frac{Nh_N\sigma(x)-1}{N-1}.
	\end{align}

	The explicit upper bound in \cite{marchal2017sub}*{Def.~1.1, p.~2, and Thm.~2.1, p.~5} gives, at every nondegenerate grid point and for every $\lambda\in\mathbb{R}$,
	\[
		\mathbb{E}_x\Bracket*{
		\exp\Parentheses*{\lambda\Parentheses*{W_A^N(x)-x}}
		}
		\le
		\exp\Parentheses*{\frac{\lambda^2}{8\Parentheses*{Na_N(x)+1}}}
		\le
		\exp\Parentheses*{\frac{\lambda^2h_N\norm{\sigma}_\infty}{4}},
	\]
	where the second inequality follows from~\eqref{eq:dir-total-concentration} and $N/(N-1)\le2$.  The same estimate is trivial in the deterministic cases.  Chernoff's bound therefore yields, for every $t>0$,
	\[
		\sup_{x\in[N^{-1}]}
		\mathbb{P}_x\Parentheses*{\abs{W_A^N(x)-x}\ge t}
		\le
		2\exp\Parentheses*{-\frac{t^2}{h_N\norm{\sigma}_\infty}}.
	\]
	 Consequently, by the moment bounds of Gaussian random variables, we have, for some $C>1$, 
	\begin{align*}
		\sup_{x\in[N^{-1}]}
		\mathbb{E}_x\Bracket*{\abs{W_A^N(x)-x}^p}
		&=
		\sup_{x\in[N^{-1}]}
		p\int_0^\infty
		t^{p-1}\mathbb{P}_x\Parentheses*{\abs{W_A^N(x)-x}\ge t}\,\mathrm dt\nonumber\\
        &\le C\mathbb{E}\left[ \abs{\mathcal N(0,h_N\norm{\sigma}_\infty)}^p\right]\\
		&\le
		C
		\norm{\sigma}_\infty^{p/2}h_N^{p/2},
	\end{align*}
	where $\mathcal N(a,b)$ denotes the normal random variable with mean $a$ and variance $b$. This proves~\eqref{eq:dir-type-moment}.

	For the remaining assertions, note that $\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(x)$ has the same law as the one-step update $X_1$ under $\mathbb{P}_x$. For some $C>0$ and all sufficiently large $N$,
	\begin{align}\label{ineq:dir-mutation-bound}
		\theta_N^++\theta_N^-  {=}h_N^2\left(h_N^{-2}\theta_N^++h_N^{-2}\theta_N^-\right) {\overset{\eqref{eq:dir-mutation-scaling}}{\le}}Ch_N^2.
	\end{align}
	For the drift term, we see by the triangular inequality  that
	\begin{align*}
		&{\qquad}\limsup_{N\to\infty}\sup_{x\in[N^{-1}]}
		\left|
		h_N^{-2}\mathbb{E}_x\Bracket*{\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(x)-x}
		-b(x)x(1-x)-\theta^+(1-x)+\theta^-x
		\right|\\
		&{\overset{\eqref{eq:dir-selection}}{\le }}
		\limsup_{N\to\infty}\Bigg[
		h_N^{-2}\sup_{x\in[N^{-1}]}
		\left|
		\mathbb{E}_x\Bracket*{\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(x)-x}
		-s_N(x)x(1-x)-\theta_N^+(1-x)+\theta_N^-x
		\right|\Bigg]\\
		&\hspace{41mm}
		+\limsup_{N \to \infty}\left(\left|h_N^{-2}\theta_N^+-\theta^+\right|
		+\left|h_N^{-2}\theta_N^--\theta^-\right|\right)
		\\
		&\overset{\eqref{eq:dir-mutation-scaling}}{=}\limsup_{N\to\infty}\Bigg[
		h_N^{-2}\sup_{x\in[N^{-1}]}
		\left|
		\mathbb{E}_x\Bracket*{\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(x)-x}
		-s_N(x)x(1-x)-\theta_N^+(1-x)+\theta_N^-x
		\right|\Bigg]\\
		&{\overset{\mathrm{Lem.}\,\ref{lem:canning_drift}}{\le}}
		\limsup_{N\to\infty}
		\Bigg[
		\norm{b}_\infty\Parentheses*{h_N\norm{\sigma}_\infty+N^{-1}}
		+2h_N^2\norm{b}_\infty^2
		+\frac14\norm{b}_\infty\Parentheses*{\theta_N^++\theta_N^-}
		\Bigg]\\
		&{\overset{\eqref{ineq:dir-mutation-bound}}{\le}}
		\limsup_{N\to\infty}\Bigg[
		\norm{b}_\infty\Parentheses*{h_N\norm{\sigma}_\infty+N^{-1}}
		+2h_N^2\norm{b}_\infty^2
		+\frac C4h_N^2\norm{b}_\infty
		\Bigg]\\
		&=0.
	\end{align*}

	\begin{samepage}
		For the variance term,
		\begin{align*}
			&\qquad\limsup_{N\to\infty}\sup_{x\in[N^{-1}]}
			\left|
			h_N^{-1}\mathbb{E}_x\Bracket*{\Parentheses*{\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(x)-x}^2}-\sigma(x)x(1-x)
			\right|\\
			&{\overset{\eqref{eq:dir-exact-calibration}}{=}}
			\limsup_{N\to\infty}h_N^{-1}\sup_{x\in[N^{-1}]}
			\left|
			\mathbb{E}_x\Bracket*{\Parentheses*{\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(x)-x}^2}-c_N(x)x(1-x)
			\right|\\
			&{\overset{\mathrm{Lem.}\,\ref{lem:canning_var}}{\le}}
			\limsup_{N\to\infty}
			\Bigg[
			\frac{h_N\norm{b}_\infty}{2N}+h_N\norm{b}_\infty
			\sqrt{\frac{h_N\norm{\sigma}_\infty}{2}}
			+\frac14h_N^3\norm{b}_\infty^2+3h_N^{-1}\Parentheses*{\theta_N^++\theta_N^-}
			\Bigg]\\
			&{\overset{\eqref{ineq:dir-mutation-bound}}{\le}}\limsup_{N\to\infty}
			\Bigg[
			\frac{h_N\norm{b}_\infty}{2N}
			+h_N\norm{b}_\infty
			\sqrt{\frac{h_N\norm{\sigma}_\infty}{2}}
			+\frac14h_N^3\norm{b}_\infty^2
			+3Ch_N
			\Bigg]\\
			&{=}0.
		\end{align*}
		Hence,~\eqref{eq:dir-coefficient-limits} holds.
	\end{samepage}

	\begin{samepage}
		For every fixed $p\ge2$ and all sufficiently large $N$,
		\begin{align*}
			\sup_{x\in[N^{-1}]}\mathbb{E}_x\Bracket*{\abs{\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(x)-x}^p}
			&{\overset{\mathrm{Lem.}\,\ref{lem:canning_higher_moments}}{\le}}
			C\Bigg(
			N^{-p/2}+h_N^{2p}\norm{b}_\infty^p+\Parentheses*{\theta_N^++\theta_N^-}^p
			+\sup_{x\in[N^{-1}]}
			\mathbb{E}_x\Bracket*{\abs{W_A^N(x)-x}^p}
			\Bigg)\\
			&{\overset{\eqref{eq:dir-type-moment}}{\le}}
			C\Parentheses*{N^{-p/2}+h_N^{2p}
			+\Parentheses*{\theta_N^++\theta_N^-}^p+h_N^{p/2}}\\
			&{\overset{\eqref{ineq:dir-mutation-bound}}{\le}}
			C\Parentheses*{N^{-p/2}+h_N^{2p}+h_N^{2p}+h_N^{p/2}}\\
			&{\overset{\eqref{eq:dir-scale-compatibility}}{\le}}
			Ch_N^{p/2},
		\end{align*}
		where we also used the fact that $h_N \le 1$ for sufficiently large $N$.  This proves~\eqref{eq:dir-update-moment}.
	\end{samepage}
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- cor-2: body_md -->
\label{cor:dirichlet-convergence}
	Fix $\alpha\in(0,1)$ and $\theta^+,\theta^-\ge0$.  Let $b\in\mathcal C([0,1];\mathbb{R})$, $\sigma\in\mathcal C([0,1];(0,\infty))$, and let $h_N\to0$ be a positive sequence satisfying \eqref{eq:dir-scale-compatibility}.  Consider the Dirichlet-weight CG models specified by~\eqref{eq:dir-target-coalescence},~\eqref{eq:dir-selection}, and~\eqref{eq:dirichlet-concentration}, with admissible mutation probabilities satisfying~\eqref{eq:dir-mutation-scaling}. Suppose that, for every $u_0\in\mathcal C(\mathbb{R};[0,1])$, the equation
	\begin{align}\label{eq:dir-limit-spde}
		\partial_tu
		&{=}\frac\alpha2\Delta u+b(u)u(1-u)
		+\theta^+(1-u)-\theta^-u
		+\sqrt{\sigma(u)u(1-u)}\,\dot W,
		\qquad u(0,\cdot)=u_0,
	\end{align}
	has a $[0,1]$-valued weak solution that is unique in law.   Let $\widetilde\xi^N$ be the corresponding spatially interpolated processes, with scalar normalization $h_N$.  If $\widetilde\xi_0^N\to u_0$ in $\mathcal C_\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{tem}}$, then $\widetilde\xi^N$ converges in distribution to the solution of \eqref{eq:dir-limit-spde} in $\mathcal D(\mathbb{R}_+;\mathcal C_\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{tem}})$.
<!-- end cor-2 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{cor:AT conditions}
	Under the preceding conditions, equation~\eqref{eq: AT original equation} admits an $[a_-,a_+]$-valued weak solution $v$ that is unique in law.  Fix $\alpha\in(0,1)$ and set $\ell:=a_+-a_-$.  Each of Corollaries~\ref{cor:pop-limit} and~\ref{cor:dirichlet-convergence} yields a sequence of mutation--selection spatial CG models, the former through varying population size and the latter through Dirichlet weights.  For either sequence, write $\widetilde\xi^N$ for its spatial interpolation.  If $\widetilde\xi_0^N\to(v_0-a_-)/\ell$ in $\mathcal C_\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{tem}}$, then
	\begin{align}\label{eq: AT approximation conclusion}
		\left(a_-+\ell\,\widetilde\xi^N_{t/\alpha}\right)_{t\ge0}\Longrightarrow (v_t)_{t\ge0}
	\end{align}
	in $\mathcal D(\mathbb{R}_+;\mathcal C_\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{tem}})$.
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
Existence of an $[a_-,a_+]$-valued weak solution follows from the construction described in~\cite{athreya2000uniqueness}*{Section~1.5, pp.~1715--1716}. It also follows from the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:canning_convergence}, see Remark~\ref{rem:existence}. Uniqueness in law follows from~\cite{athreya2000uniqueness}*{Theorem~1, p.~1716}, which states that Condition~(H2) alone is sufficient.

	For $x\in[0,1]$, set
	\begin{align*}
		B_{\mathrm{AT}}(x)
		&:=\ell^{-1}b_{\mathrm{AT}}(a_-+\ell x),
		&
		\Sigma_{\mathrm{AT}}(x)
		&:=\ell^{-2}\sigma_{\mathrm{AT}}(a_-+\ell x).
	\end{align*}
	Define
	\begin{align*}
		w_t:=\frac{v_{\alpha t}-a_-}{\ell}.
	\end{align*}
	Applying the affine normalization and the time rescaling directly to~\eqref{eq: AT original equation}, and using that the latter multiplies the space--time white-noise term in law by $\sqrt\alpha$, shows that, after denoting the rescaled white noise again by $\dot W$, the process $w$ is a weak solution of
	\begin{align}\label{eq: AT alpha scaled equation}
		\partial_t w
		=\frac\alpha2\Delta w+\alpha B_{\mathrm{AT}}(w)
		+\sqrt{\alpha\Sigma_{\mathrm{AT}}(w)}\,\dot W,
		\qquad
		w_0=\frac{v_0-a_-}{\ell}.
	\end{align}
	Conversely, if $w$ is any weak solution of~\eqref{eq: AT alpha scaled equation}, then $v_t:=a_-+\ell w_{t/\alpha}$ is a weak solution of~\eqref{eq: AT original equation}. Since every initial condition in $\mathcal C(\mathbb{R};[0,1])$ is the affine normalization of one in $\mathcal C(\mathbb{R};[a_-,a_+])$, the same correspondence establishes weak existence and uniqueness in law for~\eqref{eq: AT alpha scaled equation} for every initial condition. It remains only to realize its coefficients through the two CG constructions.

	Set
	\begin{align*}
		r_+&:=B_{\mathrm{AT}}(0)\ge0,
		&
		r_-&:=-B_{\mathrm{AT}}(1)\ge0.
	\end{align*}
	For $x\in(0,1)$, define
	\begin{align*}
		\beta(x):=
		\frac{B_{\mathrm{AT}}(x)-r_+(1-x)+r_-x}{x(1-x)}.
	\end{align*}
	The numerator admits a holomorphic extension to a complex neighborhood of $[0,1]$ and vanishes at $0$ and $1$. By~\cite{conway1978functions}*{Corollary~IV.3.9}, $\beta$ therefore extends analytically across both endpoints. Consequently,
	\begin{align}\label{eq: AT drift decomposition}
		B_{\mathrm{AT}}(x)
		=\beta(x)x(1-x)+r_+(1-x)-r_-x,
		\qquad x\in[0,1].
	\end{align}

	For $|z|=R$,~\eqref{ineq: AT H2 dominance} gives
	\begin{align*}
		\left|\sigma_{\mathrm{AT}}(z)-\sigma_2z^2\right|
		&=\left|\sum_{k\ne2}\sigma_kz^k\right|
		\le\sum_{k\ne2}|\sigma_k|R^k
		=R^2\sum_{k\ne2}|\sigma_k|R^{k-2}
		< -\sigma_2R^2
		=|\sigma_2z^2|.
	\end{align*}
	Hence, by Rouch\'e's theorem~\cite{conway1978functions}*{Theorem~V.3.8}, $\sigma_{\mathrm{AT}}$ and $\sigma_2z^2$ have the same number of zeros in $\{|z|<R\}$, namely two, counted with multiplicity. This implies that the two zeros $a_-\neq a_+$ are simple and that $\sigma_\mathrm{AT}$ has no zeros in $(a_-,a_+)$. By~\cite{conway1978functions}*{Corollary~IV.3.9}, there is therefore an analytic function $\gamma$ in a neighborhood of $[0,1]$ such that
	\begin{align}\label{eq: AT noise decomposition}
		\Sigma_{\mathrm{AT}}(x)=x(1-x)\gamma(x).
	\end{align}
	The factorization and the absence of interior zeros give $\gamma>0$ on $(0,1)$. By continuity and simplicity of the endpoint zeros, $\gamma(0)$ and $\gamma(1)$ are also positive. Hence
	\begin{align}\label{ineq: AT gamma positive}
		\min_{x\in[0,1]}\gamma(x)>0.
	\end{align}

	Define
	\begin{align*}
		b(x)&:=\alpha\beta(x),
		&
		\sigma(x)&:=\alpha\gamma(x),
		&
		\theta^+&:=\alpha r_+,
		&
		\theta^-&:=\alpha r_-.
	\end{align*}
	By~\eqref{eq: AT drift decomposition} and~\eqref{eq: AT noise decomposition},
	\begin{gather*}
		b(x)x(1-x)+\theta^+(1-x)-\theta^-x
		=\alpha B_{\mathrm{AT}}(x),\\
		\sigma(x)x(1-x)
		=\alpha\Sigma_{\mathrm{AT}}(x).
	\end{gather*}
	Thus~\eqref{eq: AT alpha scaled equation} is precisely the limiting equation in Corollaries~\ref{cor:pop-limit} and~\ref{cor:dirichlet-convergence} for these coefficients.

	For the varying-population construction, since $b$ and $\sigma$ are continuous and $\sigma>0$ on $[0,1]$, Corollary~\ref{cor:pop-limit} applies. For the Dirichlet construction, take $h_N=N^{-1/2}$ and, for all sufficiently large $N$, set $\theta_N^\pm=h_N^2\theta^\pm$, choosing the finitely many remaining mutation probabilities subject to admissibility. Moreover,
	\begin{align*}
		\frac1{Nh_N}
		&=\frac1{\sqrt N}
		\le\alpha\min_{x\in[0,1]}\gamma(x)
		=\min_{x\in[0,1]}\sigma(x)
	\end{align*}
	for all sufficiently large $N$, where the inequality follows from~\eqref{ineq: AT gamma positive}. Hence~\eqref{eq:dir-scale-compatibility} holds, and Corollary~\ref{cor:dirichlet-convergence} applies. Under either construction, $\widetilde\xi^N$ converges in distribution to $w$ in $\mathcal D(\mathbb{R}_+;\mathcal C_\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{tem}})$. The map $f\mapsto a_-+\ell f_{\cdot/\alpha}$ is continuous on this path space, so the continuous mapping theorem gives~\eqref{eq: AT approximation conclusion}.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:index exchangeable}
	Let $N\ge2$ and $x\in[N^{-1}]$. For $1\le i,j\le N$ with $i\ne j$, exchangeability of $\mathcal W(x)$ gives
	\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{E}_x[w_i(x)]=\frac1N,\qquad
		\mathbb{E}_x[w_i(x)^2]=\frac{c_N(x)}N,\qquad
		\mathbb{E}_x[w_i(x)w_j(x)]=\frac{1-c_N(x)}{N(N-1)}.
	\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
By exchangeability, the normalization of the weights, and~\eqref{eq:cn scaling},
	\begin{align*}
		1 =\mathbb{E}_x\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^N w_\ell(x)\right]
		=N\mathbb{E}_x[w_i(x)],\qquad
		c_N(x)
		=\mathbb{E}_x\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^N w_\ell(x)^2\right]
		=N\mathbb{E}_x[w_i(x)^2].
	\end{align*}
	Moreover,
	\begin{align*}
		1
		&=\mathbb{E}_x\left[\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^Nw_\ell(x)\right)^2\right]\\
		&=\mathbb{E}_x\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^Nw_\ell(x)^2\right]
		+\mathbb{E}_x\left[\sum_{\substack{\ell,m=1\\\ell\ne m}}^N
		w_\ell(x)w_m(x)\right]\\
		&=c_N(x)+N(N-1)\mathbb{E}_x[w_i(x)w_j(x)],
	\end{align*}
	where we used exchangeability of $\mathcal W(x)$ in the last equality. Solving for $\mathbb{E}_x[w_i(x)w_j(x)]$ gives the last identity.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:wa var}
	For each $N\ge2$ and $x\in[N^{-1}]$,
	\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{E}_x\left[W_A^N(x)\left(1-W_A^N(x)\right)\right]
		&=x(1-x)-\frac{N}{N-1}x(1-x)
		\left(c_N(x)-\frac1N\right),\\
		\mathbb{E}_x\left[\left(W_A^N(x)-x\right)^2\right]
		&=x(1-x)\frac{N}{N-1}
		\left(c_N(x)-\frac1N\right).
	\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
Since $I_x$ is deterministic and has cardinality $Nx$,
	\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{E}_x[W_A^N(x)]
		&=\sum_{i\in I_x}\mathbb{E}_x[w_i(x)]
		\overset{\mathrm{Lem.}\,\ref{lem:index exchangeable}}{=}\frac{Nx}{N}
		=x,
	\end{align*}
	and
	\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{E}_x\left[W_A^N(x)^2\right]
		&=\sum_{i\in I_x}\mathbb{E}_x[w_i(x)^2]
		+\sum_{\substack{i,j\in I_x\\i\ne j}}
		\mathbb{E}_x[w_i(x)w_j(x)]\\
		&\overset{\mathrm{Lem.}\,\ref{lem:index exchangeable}}{=}
		Nx\frac{c_N(x)}N
		+Nx(Nx-1)\frac{1-c_N(x)}{N(N-1)}.
	\end{align*}
	Consequently,
	\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{E}_x\left[\left(W_A^N(x)-x\right)^2\right]
		&=\mathbb{E}_x\left[W_A^N(x)^2\right]-x^2\\
		&=xc_N(x)+\frac{x(Nx-1)}{N-1}\left(1-c_N(x)\right)-x^2\\
		&=x(1-x)\frac{Nc_N(x)-1}{N-1}\\
		&=x(1-x)\frac{N}{N-1}
		\left(c_N(x)-\frac1N\right).
	\end{align*}
	Finally,
	\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{E}_x\left[W_A^N(x)\left(1-W_A^N(x)\right)\right]
		&=x-\mathbb{E}_x\left[W_A^N(x)^2\right]\\
		&=x-\left(
		\mathbb{E}_x\left[\left(W_A^N(x)-x\right)^2\right]+x^2\right)\\
		&=x(1-x)-\frac{N}{N-1}x(1-x)
		\left(c_N(x)-\frac1N\right).
	\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:canning_drift}
	Let $N\ge2$ and suppose $\abs{s_N(x)}<1/2$ for all $x \in [N^{-1}]$, then the following  holds
	\begin{align*}
		&\abs{\mathbb E_x\left[X_1-x\right]
		-s_N(x)x(1-x)-\theta_N^+(1-x)+\theta_N^-x}
		\\
		\le&
		\abs{s_N(x)}\Parentheses*{c_N(x)+N^{-1}}
		+2\abs{s_N(x)}^2
		+\frac14\abs{s_N(x)}(\theta_N^++\theta_N^-),
		\qquad x \in [N^{-1}].
	\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
We first note that by the conditional binomial law, we have
	\begin{align}\label{eq:canning_drift_1}
		\mathbb{E}_x\left[X_1-x\right]&=\mathbb{E}_x\left[\mathbb{E}_x\left[X_1-x\mid\mathcal W(x)\right]\right]\nonumber\\
		&=\mathbb{E}_x\left[\widehat P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))-x\right]\nonumber\\
		&{\overset{\eqref{eq: mutation adjusted probability}}{=}}(1-\theta_N^+-\theta_N^-)\mathbb{E}_x \left[ \left(P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))-x\right)\right]+\theta_N^+(1-x)-\theta_N^-x.
	\end{align}
	By Lemma~\ref{lem:wa var}, we have
	\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{E}_x\left[P_N(x,\mathcal W(x)) - x \right]&{\overset{\eqref{eq:tylor expansion}}{=}}\mathbb{E}_x\left[W_A^N(x)-x+s_N(x)W_A^N(x)(1-W_A^N(x))+\rho_N(x)\right]\\
		&=s_N(x)\mathbb{E}_x\left[W_A^N(x)(1-W_A^N(x))\right]+\mathbb{E}_x\left[\rho_N(x)\right]\\
		&{\overset{\mathrm{Lem.}\,\ref{lem:wa var}}{=}}s_N(x)x(1-x)
		\left(1-\frac{N}{N-1}\left(c_N(x)-\frac1N\right)\right)
		+\mathbb{E}_x\left[\rho_N(x)\right].
	\end{align*}
	Therefore,
	\begin{align}
		&\abs{\mathbb{E}_x[P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))-x]-s_N(x)x(1-x)}\notag\\
		&\quad\le
		\abs{s_N(x)}\frac{N}{N-1}x(1-x)
		\abs{c_N(x)-N^{-1}}+\mathbb{E}_x\left[\abs{\rho_N(x)}\right]\notag\\
		&\quad\le
		\abs{s_N(x)}\Parentheses*{c_N(x)+N^{-1}}+2\abs{s_N(x)}^2.
		\label{ineq:canning_drift_2}
	\end{align}
	Here we used $\frac{N}{N-1}x(1-x)\le1$ for all $N \ge 2$, $x \in [0,1]$ and~\eqref{ineq:canning_taylor_error} in the last line. Hence,
	\begin{align*}
		&\qquad\abs{\mathbb{E}_x\left[X_1-x\right]-s_N(x)x(1-x)
		-\theta_N^+(1-x)+\theta_N^-x}\\
		&\overset{\eqref{eq:canning_drift_1}}{=}
		\abs{(1-\theta_N^+-\theta_N^-)
		\mathbb{E}_x\left[P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))-x\right]
		-s_N(x)x(1-x)}\\
		&=
		\abs{(1-\theta_N^+-\theta_N^-)
		\left(\mathbb{E}_x\left[P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))-x\right]
		-s_N(x)x(1-x)\right)
		-(\theta_N^++\theta_N^-)s_N(x)x(1-x)}\\
		&\le(1-\theta_N^+-\theta_N^-)
		\abs{\mathbb{E}_x\left[P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))-x\right]
		-s_N(x)x(1-x)}
		+(\theta_N^++\theta_N^-)\abs{s_N(x)}x(1-x)\\
		&\overset{\eqref{ineq:canning_drift_2}}{\le}
		\abs{s_N(x)}\Parentheses*{c_N(x)+N^{-1}}
		+2\abs{s_N(x)}^2
		+\frac14\abs{s_N(x)}(\theta_N^++\theta_N^-),
	\end{align*}
	where the last inequality follows from $\theta_N^++\theta_N^-\le1$ and $x(1-x)\le1/4$.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lem:canning_var}
	Let $N\ge2$ and suppose $\abs{s_N(x)}<1/2$ for all $x \in [N^{-1}]$, then the following  holds
	\begin{align*}
		\abs{\mathbb{E}_x\left[\Parentheses*{X_1-x}^2\right]-x(1-x)c_N(x)}
		\le
		\frac{\abs{s_N(x)}}{2N}
		+\abs{s_N(x)}\sqrt{\frac{c_N(x)}2}
		+\frac{\abs{s_N(x)}^2}{4}
		+3(\theta_N^++\theta_N^-).
	\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
Note, it holds for all $x \in [N^{-1}]$ that
	\begin{align}\label{eq:canning_var_step1}
		\mathbb{E}_x\left[\Parentheses*{X_1-x}^2\mid\mathcal W(x)\right]
		\overset{\eqref{eq:canning_conditional_binomial}}{=}\frac{\widehat P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))
		\left(1-\widehat P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))\right)}N
		+\left(\widehat P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))-x\right)^2.
	\end{align}
	Set
	\[
		F_N(y,x):=\frac{y(1-y)}N+(y-x)^2.
	\]
	For $x,y,y'\in[0,1]$,
	\begin{align}\label{ineq: mutation variance comparison}
		\abs{F_N(y,x)-F_N(y',x)}
		&\le \frac1N\abs{y-y'}+2\abs{y-y'}
		\le3\abs{y-y'}.
	\end{align}
	Hence, by the triangular inequality, it holds for all $N \ge 2$ and $x \in [N^{-1}]$ that
	\begin{align*}
		\abs{\mathbb{E}_x\left[\Parentheses*{X_1-x}^2 \right] - x(1-x)c_N(x)}&{\overset{\eqref{eq:canning_var_step1}}{=}} \abs{\mathbb{E}_x\left[F_N(\widehat{P}_N(x,\mathcal W(x)),x)\right]-x(1-x)c_N(x)}\\
		&\le \abs{\mathbb{E}_x\left[F_N(\widehat{P}_N(x,\mathcal W(x)),x)-F_N(P_N(x,\mathcal W(x)),x)\right]}\\
		&\qquad + \abs{\mathbb{E}_x \left[F_N(P_N(x,\mathcal W(x)),x) - x(1-x)c_N(x) \right]}\\
		&{\overset{\eqref{ineq: mutation variance comparison}}{\le}}3\mathbb{E}_x\left[\abs{\widehat{P}_N(x,\mathcal W(x))-P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))}\right]\\
		&\qquad +\abs{\mathbb{E}_x \left[F_N(P_N(x,\mathcal W(x)),x) - x(1-x)c_N(x) \right]}\\
		&{\overset{\eqref{ineq: mutation probability difference}}{\le}}3(\theta_N^++\theta_N^-)+\abs{\mathbb{E}_x \left[F_N(P_N(x,\mathcal W(x)),x) - x(1-x)c_N(x) \right]}.
	\end{align*}
	Therefore, it is enough to show
	\begin{align}\label{ineq:canning_var_reduction_target}
		\abs{\mathbb{E}_x \left[F_N(P_N(x,\mathcal W(x)),x) - x(1-x)c_N(x) \right]}
		\le
		\frac{\abs{s_N(x)}}{2N}
		+\abs{s_N(x)}\sqrt{\frac{c_N(x)}2}
		+\frac{\abs{s_N(x)}^2}{4}.
	\end{align}

	Next, consider the following identities,
	\begin{align*}
		&P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))
		\left(1-P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))\right)
		-W_A^N(x)\left(1-W_A^N(x)\right)\\
		&\qquad
		=\left(P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))-W_A^N(x)\right)
		\left(1-2W_A^N(x)\right)
		-\left(P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))-W_A^N(x)\right)^2,\\
		&\left(P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))-x\right)^2
		-\left(W_A^N(x)-x\right)^2\\
		&\qquad
		=2\left(W_A^N(x)-x\right)
		\left(P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))-W_A^N(x)\right)
		+\left(P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))-W_A^N(x)\right)^2.
	\end{align*}
	Lemma~\ref{lem:wa var} yields
	\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{E}_x\left[F_N(W_A^N(x),x)\right]
		=x(1-x)c_N(x).
	\end{align*}
	This implies, for all $x \in [N^{-1}]$,
	\begin{align}\label{eq:canning_var_error_remainder}
		\mathbb{E}_x\left[F_N(P_N(x,\mathcal W(x)),x)\right]-x(1-x)c_N(x)
		&=\mathbb{E}_x\left[F_N(W_A^N(x),x) \right]-x(1-x)c_N(x)\nonumber\\
		&\qquad + \mathbb{E}_x\left[F_N(P_N(x,\mathcal W(x)),x) - F_N(W_A^N(x),x) \right]\nonumber\\
		&=r_{N,1}(x)+r_{N,2}(x)+r_{N,3}(x),
	\end{align}
	where
	\begin{align*}
		r_{N,1}(x)
		&:=\frac1N\mathbb{E}_x\left[
		\left(P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))-W_A^N(x)\right)
		\left(1-2W_A^N(x)\right)
		\right],\\
		r_{N,2}(x)
		&:=2\mathbb{E}_x\left[
		\left(W_A^N(x)-x\right)
		\left(P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))-W_A^N(x)\right)
		\right],\\
		r_{N,3}(x)
		&:=\left(1-\frac1N\right)
		\mathbb{E}_x\left[
		\left(P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))-W_A^N(x)\right)^2
		\right].
	\end{align*}
	For the error terms, we see by~\eqref{ineq:canning_taylor_error} that
	\begin{align*}
		\abs{r_{N,1}(x)}
		\le\frac{\abs{s_N(x)}}{2N},\text{ and }
		\abs{r_{N,3}(x)}
		\le\frac{\abs{s_N(x)}^2}{4},\qquad \text{ for all }x \in [N^{-1}].
	\end{align*}
	For $r_{N,2}$ term, consider by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality that
	\begin{align*}
		\abs{r_{N,2}(x)}
		&\le2 \norm{W_A^N(x) - x}_{L^2_\Omega} \norm{P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))-W_A^N(x)}_{L^2_\Omega}\\
		&{\overset{\eqref{ineq:canning_taylor_error}}{\le} }\norm{W_A^N(x) - x}_{L^2_\Omega} \abs{s_N(x)}\\
		&{\overset{\mathrm{Lem.}\,\ref{lem:wa var}}{=}}\Parentheses*{x(1-x)\frac{N}{N-1}\left(c_N(x)-\frac1N\right)}^\frac{1}{2}\abs{s_N(x)}\\
		&\overset{\mathrm{Lem.}\,\ref{lem:wf-floor}}{\le}\abs{s_N(x)}\sqrt{\frac{c_N(x)}2}.
	\end{align*}
	Inserting the bounds for $r_{N,1}$, $r_{N,2}$, and $r_{N,3}$ into~\eqref{eq:canning_var_error_remainder} gives~\eqref{ineq:canning_var_reduction_target}, which completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lem:canning_higher_moments}
	Let $p\ge2$, $N\ge2$, and $x\in[N^{-1}]$, and suppose $\abs{s_N(x)}<1/2$. Then
	\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{E}_x \left[\abs{X_1 - x}^p \right]
		\lesssim
		N^{-p/2}+\abs{s_N(x)}^p
		+(\theta_N^++\theta_N^-)^p
		+\mathbb{E}_x\left[\abs{W_A^N(x)-x}^p\right].
	\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
Let $p \ge 2$, we see by the triangle inequality, it holds that
	\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{E}_x\left[\abs{X_1-x}^p\right]
		&\lesssim
		\mathbb{E}_x\left[
		\mathbb{E}_x\left[\abs{X_1-\widehat P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))}^p
		\mid\mathcal W(x)\right]
		+\abs{\widehat P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))-x}^p\right]\\
		&{\overset{\eqref{eq:canning_conditional_binomial}}{\lesssim}}
		N^{-p/2}+\mathbb{E}_x\left[\abs{\widehat P_N(x,\mathcal W(x))-x}^p\right]\\
		&\lesssim N^{-p/2}+\mathbb{E}_x\left[\abs{W_A^N(x)-x}^p\right]+\mathbb{E}_x\left[\abs{W_A^N(x) - \widehat{P}_N(x,\mathcal W(x)) }^p \right]\\
		&{\overset{\eqref{ineq: mutation probability difference}}{\lesssim}} N^{-p/2}
		+\mathbb{E}_x\left[\abs{W_A^N(x) - x}^p \right]
		+\abs{s_N(x)}^p +\Parentheses*{\theta_N^++\theta_N^-}^p,
	\end{align*}
	which is the desired result.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lem:canning_unbiased_transfer}
	Under the assumptions of Theorem~\ref{thm:canning_convergence},
	\begin{align}\label{eq:mean_uniform_rv_canning_cov}
		\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{x\in[0,1]}
		\left|h_N^{-2}\mathbb{E}_x[\overline{\mathcal R}^N(x)-x]
		-B(x)\right|=0.
	\end{align}
	Moreover,
	\begin{align}\label{eq:var_uniform_rv_canning_cov}
		\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{x\in[0,1]}
		\left|h_N^{-1}\mathbb{E}_x[(\overline{\mathcal R}^N(x)-x)^2]
		-\sigma(x)x(1-x)\right|=0.
	\end{align}
	With $p>8$ and $\varepsilon>0$ as in~\eqref{ineq:p_moment_canning_thm}, it holds that
	\[
		\sup_{x\in[0,1]}\mathbb{E}_x\left[|\overline{\mathcal R}^N(x)-x|^p\right]
		\lesssim h_N^{1+\kappa_p},
		\qquad \kappa_p:=\min\{p/2,4+\varepsilon\}-1>3.
	\]
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
The limits in~\eqref{eq:bsigma def} and the fact that $c_N(z)\ge N^{-1}$ imply that, for some $C>0$ and all sufficiently large $N$,
	\begin{align}\label{ineq:canning_uniform_initial_bounds}
		\sup_{z\in[N^{-1}]}|s_N(z)|\le Ch_N^2,
		\qquad N^{-1}\le\sup_{z\in[0,1]}c_N(z)\le(\|\sigma\|_\infty+1)h_N,
		\qquad
		\theta_N^++\theta_N^-\le Ch_N^2.
	\end{align}
	In particular, $|s_N(z)|<1/2$ for all $z\in[N^{-1}]$ and all sufficiently large $N$, so Lemmas~\ref{lem:canning_drift}--\ref{lem:canning_higher_moments} apply. Recall the uniform variable $V$ defining $U_N$.  Since $\mathbb{E}[U_N(x)]=x$, it holds for all $x \in [0,1]$ and $N \in \mathbb{N}$ that
	\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{E}_x[\overline{\mathcal R}^N(x)-x]
		&=\mathbb{E}\left[
		\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(U_N(x))-U_N(x)\mid V\right]
		\right]
		+\mathbb{E}[U_N(x)-x]\\
		&=\mathbb{E}\left[
		\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(U_N(x))-U_N(x)\mid V\right]
		\right].
	\end{align*}
	Adding and subtracting $B(U_N(x))$ now yields
	\begin{align*}
		h_N^{-2}\mathbb{E}_x[\overline{\mathcal R}^N(x)-x]
		-B(x)
		=\mathbb{E}[
		h_N^{-2}\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(U_N(x))
		-U_N(x)\mid V]
		-B(U_N(x))
		]
		+\mathbb{E}[B(U_N(x))-B(x)].
	\end{align*}
	Hence, by the triangular inequality, it holds for $N \in \mathbb{N}$ that
	\begin{align*}
		\sup_{x\in[0,1]}
		\left|h_N^{-2}\mathbb{E}_x[\overline{\mathcal R}^N(x)-x]-B(x)\right|
		&\overset{\eqref{ineq:uniform_rv_estimates}}{\le} \sup_{z\in[N^{-1}]}
		\left|h_N^{-2}\mathbb{E}_z[\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(z)-z]-B(z)\right|\\
		&\qquad+\sup_{\substack{x\in[0,1],\ z\in[N^{-1}]\\|x-z|\le N^{-1}}}
		|B(z)-B(x)|.
	\end{align*}
	The first conclusion follows from Lemma~\ref{lem:canning_drift},~\eqref{eq:bsigma def}, and uniform continuity of $B$ on $[0,1]$.

	For the second moment, conditioning on $V$ and adding and subtracting $\sigma(U_N(x))U_N(x)(1-U_N(x))$ gives
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:canning_unbiased_transfer_var}
		\begin{aligned}
			&h_N^{-1}\mathbb{E}_x[(\overline{\mathcal R}^N(x)-x)^2]
			-\sigma(x)x(1-x)\\
			&=h_N^{-1} \mathbb{E}\left[\Parentheses*{\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(U_N(x)) - U_N(x) + U_N(x) - x}^2\right]-\sigma(x)x(1-x)\\
			&=\mathbb{E}\left[
			h_N^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(
			U_N(x))-U_N(x))^2\mid V\right]
			-\sigma(U_N(x))U_N(x)(1-U_N(x))
			\right]\\
			&\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma(U_N(x))
			U_N(x)(1-U_N(x))
			-\sigma(x)x(1-x)\right]\\
			&\quad+\frac2{h_N}\mathbb{E}\left[(U_N(x)-x)
			\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(U_N(x))
			-U_N(x)\mid V\right]\right]\\
			&\quad+\frac1{h_N}\mathbb{E}[(U_N(x)-x)^2].
		\end{aligned}
	\end{equation}
	By Lemma~\ref{lem:canning_var}, it holds that
	\begin{align*}
		&\abs{\mathbb{E}\left[
		h_N^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(
		U_N(x))-U_N(x))^2\mid V\right]
		-\sigma(U_N(x))U_N(x)(1-U_N(x))
		\right]} \\
		\le &\sup_{z \in [N^{-1}]}
		\abs{h_N^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[(\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(z)-z)^2\right]
		-\sigma(z)z(1-z)}\\
		\le &\sup_{z \in [N^{-1}]}\abs{h_N^{-1}\Parentheses*{\mathbb{E} \left[ (\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(z)-z)^2\right] - z(1-z)c_N(z)} }+ \sup_{z \in [N^{-1}]}\abs{z(1-z)\frac{c_N(z)}{h_N} - \sigma(z)z(1-z)}\\
		\le & \sup_{z \in [N^{-1}]}\Parentheses*{\frac{\abs{s_N(z)}}{2Nh_N}
		+\abs{s_N(z)}\sqrt{\frac{c_N(z)}2}h_N^{-1}
		+\frac{\abs{s_N(z)}^2}{4h_N}
		+3(\theta_N^++\theta_N^-)h_N^{-1}}
		+ \sup_{z \in [N^{-1}]}\abs{\frac{c_N(z)}{h_N} - \sigma(z)}\\
		\overset{\eqref{ineq:canning_uniform_initial_bounds}}{\le}& C\Parentheses*{N^{-1}h_N + h_N^{\frac{3}{2}} + h_N^3+h_N} + \sup_{z \in [N^{-1}]}\abs{\frac{c_N(z)}{h_N} - \sigma(z)}.
	\end{align*}
	Also,
	\begin{align*}
		\sup_{x \in [0,1]}
		\abs{\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma(U_N(x))U_N(x)(1-U_N(x))
		-\sigma(x)x(1-x)\right]}
		&\le \sup_{\substack{x,z \in [0,1], \\|x-z|\le N^{-1}}}
		\abs{\sigma(z)z(1-z)-\sigma(x)x(1-x)}.
	\end{align*}
	By the conditional binomial law, $\mathbb{E}_z[W_A^N(z)]=z$, and \eqref{ineq:canning_taylor_error} and \eqref{ineq: mutation probability difference}, it holds that
	\begin{align*}
		\frac2{h_N}\abs{\mathbb{E}\left[(U_N(x)-x)\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(U_N(x))-U_N(x)\mid V\right]\right]}&
		{\overset{\eqref{ineq:uniform_rv_estimates}}{\le}}
		\frac{2}{h_NN}\sup_{z\in[N^{-1}]}\abs{\mathbb{E}_z[\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(z)-z]}\\
		&\le\frac{2}{h_NN}\Parentheses*{\frac12
		\sup_{z\in[N^{-1}]}\abs{s_N(z)}+\theta_N^+ +\theta_N^-}\\
		&{\overset{\eqref{ineq:canning_uniform_initial_bounds}}{\le}} Ch_N^2.
	\end{align*}
	Lastly, there is some $C>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $N$,
	\[
		\sup_{x \in [0,1]}h_N^{-1}\mathbb{E}[(U_N(x)-x)^2]
		\overset{\eqref{ineq:uniform_rv_estimates}}{\le}
		N^{-2}h_N^{-1}
		\overset{\eqref{ineq:canning_uniform_initial_bounds}}{\le} C N^{-1}.
	\]
	Inserting these estimates into~\eqref{eq:canning_unbiased_transfer_var}, there is some $C>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $N$,
	\begin{align*}
		\sup_{x \in [0,1]}\abs{h_N^{-1}\mathbb{E}_x[(\overline{\mathcal R}^N(x)-x)^2]-\sigma(x)x(1-x)}
		&\le C \Parentheses*{h_N+N^{-1}} +\sup_{z \in [N^{-1}]}\abs{\frac{c_N(z)}{h_N} - \sigma(z)}\\
		&\qquad+ \sup_{\substack{x,z \in [0,1], \\|x-z|\le N^{-1}}}\abs{\sigma(z)z(1-z)-\sigma(x)x(1-x)},
	\end{align*}
	which converges to $0$ as $N \to \infty$ by~\eqref{eq:bsigma def} and uniform continuity of $\sigma$ on $[0,1]$. This proves~\eqref{eq:var_uniform_rv_canning_cov}.

	Finally, by the definition of $\overline{\mathcal R}^N$ and Lemma~\ref{lem:canning_higher_moments}, it holds uniformly for sufficiently large $N \in \mathbb{N}$ that
	\begin{align*}
		\sup_{x\in[0,1]}\mathbb{E}_x\left[\abs{\overline{\mathcal R}^N(x)-x}^p\right]
		&{=}\sup_{x\in[0,1]}\mathbb{E}\left[
		\abs{\Parentheses*{\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(U_N(x))
		-U_N(x)}
		+\Parentheses*{U_N(x)-x}}^p\right]\\
		&\le 2^{p-1}\sup_{x\in[0,1]}\mathbb{E}\left[
		\abs{\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(U_N(x))
		-U_N(x)}^p
		+\abs{U_N(x)-x}^p\right]\\
		&{\overset{\eqref{ineq:uniform_rv_estimates}}{\le}} 2^{p-1}\left(
		\sup_{z\in[N^{-1}]}\mathbb{E}_z\left[\abs{\widetilde{\mathcal R}^N(z)-z}^p\right]
		+N^{-p}\right)\\
		&{\overset{\mathrm{Lem.}\,\ref{lem:canning_higher_moments}}{\lesssim}}
		N^{-p/2}+\sup_{z\in[N^{-1}]}\abs{s_N(z)}^p
		+(\theta_N^++\theta_N^-)^p\\
		&\quad+\sup_{z\in[N^{-1}]}\mathbb{E}_z\left[\abs{W_A^N(z)-z}^p\right]+N^{-p}\\
		&{\overset{\substack{\eqref{ineq:canning_uniform_initial_bounds}}}{\lesssim}}
		h_N^{p/2}+h_N^{2p}
		+h_N^{4+\varepsilon}+h_N^p\\
		&\lesssim h_N^{\min\{p/2,4+\varepsilon\}},
	\end{align*}
	where we also used~\eqref{ineq:p_moment_canning_thm} in the fifth line. This completes the proof of the lemma.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
We use Theorem~\ref{thm:main}, which is stated along a sequence $L\to\infty$: an $N$-indexed sequence of pairs $(L_N,\mathcal R_N)$ with $L_N>0$ and $L_N\to\infty$ may replace $(L,\mathcal R^L)$ throughout its hypotheses and conclusion. Set
	\[
		L_N:=h_N^{-1},
		\qquad
		\mathcal R_N:=\overline{\mathcal R}^N.
	\]
	Lemma~\ref{lem:canning_unbiased_transfer} gives
	\begin{align*}
		\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{x\in[0,1]}
		\abs{h_N^{-2}\mathbb{E}_x\Bracket*{\mathcal R_N(x)-x}
		-B(x)}&=0,\\
		\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{x\in[0,1]}
		\abs{h_N^{-1}\mathbb{E}_x\Bracket*{\Parentheses*{\mathcal R_N(x)-x}^2}
		-\sigma(x)x(1-x)}&=0.
	\end{align*}
	In terms of the coefficients defined in~\eqref{eq:R assumption}, these estimates give~\eqref{eq:coefficient convergence}, with limiting coefficients $B(x)$ and $\sigma(x)x(1-x)$, respectively. Since $B(0)=\theta^+\ge0$, $B(1)=-\theta^-\le0$, and $\sigma(x)x(1-x)$ vanishes at $x=0,1$, the boundary assumptions of Theorem~\ref{thm:main} hold. The third conclusion of Lemma~\ref{lem:canning_unbiased_transfer} gives~\eqref{ineq:p_moment bound} with $\kappa_p>3$. Hence the desired result follows from Theorem~\ref{thm:main}.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- prop-5: body_md -->
\label{prop:lawler-242}
	Suppose $p_n$ is the $n$-step transition probability matrix of the above described random walk on $\mathbb{Z}$, then
	\begin{align}\label{ineq:discrete-heat-kernel-gradient-abs-difference}
		\sum_{z \in \mathbb{Z}} \abs{p_n(z) - p_n(z+y)} \le c\abs{y}n^{-1/2},
	\end{align}
	and
	\begin{align*}
		p_n(0) \le c n^{-1/2}.
	\end{align*}
<!-- end prop-5 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{cor:heat kernel on diagonal bound}
	Uniformly in $n \in \mathbb{N}$,
	\[
		\sup_{x,z} p_n(x,z) \lesssim n^{-\frac{1}{2}}.
	\]
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
See Appendix~\ref{app:heat-kernel-proofs}.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- corollary-3: body_md -->
\label{cor:gradient l2 bound}
	Uniformly in $x,y \in \mathbb{Z}$ and $n \in \mathbb{N}$,
	\begin{align*}
		\sum_z \Parentheses*{p_n(x,z) - p_n(y,z)}^2 \lesssim \min\left\{n^{-1/2}\frac{\abs{x-y}}{n^{1/2}}, n^{-1/2}\right\}.
	\end{align*}
	Moreover, uniformly in $x\in\mathbb{Z}$ and $n,h\ge1$,
	\begin{align}\label{ineq:heat kernel temporal l1 difference}
		\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}}\abs{p_{n+h}(x,z)-p_n(x,z)}
		\lesssim\min\left\{1,\sqrt{\frac{h}{n}}\right\}.
	\end{align}
<!-- end corollary-3 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
See Appendix~\ref{app:heat-kernel-proofs}.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lem:local clt}
	There exists $c>0$ and for every $k \ge 4$ there is a $c(k)< \infty$ such that for all integers $n >0$ and $x \in \mathbb{Z}$, the following holds with $z:= x/ \sqrt{n}$:
	\begin{equation}\label{ineq: local clt useful}
		\bigl|p_n(x)-\overline p_n(x)\bigr|
		\le  \frac{c(k)}{n^{3/2}}\Parentheses*{(|z|^k+1)\,e^{-\frac{z^2}{2\alpha} }+\frac{1}{n^{(k-3)/2}}}.
	\end{equation}
	\begin{equation}
		\bigl|p_n(x)-\overline p_n(x)\bigr|
		\le \frac{c}{n^{3/2}|z|^2} .
	\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lem: heat kernel temporal l2 difference bound}
	Let $p_n(x,y)$ be the discrete heat kernel on $\mathbb{Z}$. Then, uniformly in $n,k \in \mathbb{N}$,
	\begin{align*}
		\sum_y\Parentheses*{p_{n+k}(x,y) - p_{n}(x,y)}^2 \lesssim \frac{k}{n^{3/2}}.
	\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
See Appendix~\ref{app:heat-kernel-proofs}.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lem:weighted discrete semigroup stability}
	For $\lambda,T>0$ and $L\in\mathbb{N}$, define for  $f: \mathbb{Z}/L \to \mathbb{R}$ that
	\[
		\norm{f}_{(\lambda),L}
		:=\sup_{x_i\in\mathbb{Z}/L}e^{-\lambda|x_i|}\abs{f(x_i)}.
	\]
	For $k\in\mathbb{Z}_+$ and $x_i\in\mathbb{Z}/L$, let $P_k^Lf(x_i):=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}p_k(Lx_i,j)f(j/L)$.
	Then, for every $f$ with $\norm{f}_{(\lambda),L}<\infty$ and every $k\in\mathbb{Z}_+$ satisfying $k\le TL^2$,
	\[
		\norm{P_k^Lf}_{(\lambda),L}
		\le
		2\exp\left\{\frac{\alpha T\lambda^2}{2}\cosh\lambda\right\}
		\norm{f}_{(\lambda),L}.
	\]
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
See Appendix~\ref{app:heat-kernel-proofs}.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- prop-6: body_md -->
\label{prop:spatial_increment_moment_bound}
	For any $T>0$, the following inequality holds uniformly for sufficiently large $L \ge 1$, $0<t\le T$ and $x_i,x_j \in \mathbb{Z}/L$ with $|x_i - x_j| \le 1$,
	\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{E}\left[ \abs{I_2^L(t,x_i)-I_2^{L}(t,x_j)}^p\right] +  \mathbb{E}\left[ \abs{I_3^L(t,x_i)-I_3^{L}(t,x_j)}^p\right]\le C \abs{x_i - x_j}^{\frac{p}{4}\wedge (1+\kappa_p)}.
	\end{align*}
<!-- end prop-6 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
{ By~\eqref{eq:coefficient convergence}, there exist $L_0>1$ and $C>0$ such that, for all $L\ge L_0$, $t\ge0$, and $x_i\in\mathbb{Z}/L$,
	\begin{align*}
		\abs{B_L\Parentheses*{\xi_t^L}(x_i)}\le C.
	\end{align*}}
	If $t<L^{-2}$, then $\lfloor tL^2\rfloor=0$ and $I^L_2(t,\cdot)=I^L_3(t,\cdot)=0$; the claim is also trivial if $x_i=x_j$. Hence we assume $t\ge L^{-2}$ and $x_i\neq x_j$, so that $|x_i-x_j|\ge L^{-1}$. In the sums over $k$ below, the term $k=\lfloor tL^2\rfloor-1$ has kernel $q^L_0$, the identity kernel.
	Hence there is some $C>0$ so that for all $L\ge L_0$, $i,j \in \mathbb{Z}$ and $t>0$
	\begin{align*}
		\abs{I^L_2(t,x_i) - I^L_2(t,x_j)}^p  &\le \Parentheses*{L^{-2}\sum_{k = 0}^{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor-1}\sum_\ell\abs{q^L_{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor - 1 -k}(x_i,x_\ell) - q^L_{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor - 1 -k}(x_j,x_\ell)}}^p\\
		&\overset{\eqref{ineq:discrete-heat-kernel-gradient-abs-difference}}{\le} C\Parentheses*{2L^{-2}+L^{-2} \sum_{k =0}^{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor - 2} \frac{\abs{x_i - x_j}}{\sqrt{(\lfloor tL^2\rfloor-1-k)/L^2}}}^p\\
		&\le C\Parentheses*{L^{-2}+\abs{x_i - x_j}\int_0^t \frac{1}{\sqrt{t-s}}\mathrm ds}^p\\
		&\le C |x_i -x_j|^p.
	\end{align*}
	Here $2L^{-2}$ is from when $k=\lfloor tL^2\rfloor-1$, for which $\sum_\ell|q^L_0(x_i,x_\ell)-q^L_0(x_j,x_\ell)|=2$, and in the last line we used $L^{-2}\le L^{-1}\le|x_i-x_j|$.

	On the other hand, by Rosenthal's inequality (cf.\ \cite{hall2014martingale}*{Theorem 2.12}), we see it holds uniformly in $t>0$ and $x_i,x_j \in \mathbb{Z}/L$ that
	\begin{align*}
		&\mathbb{E}\left[ \abs{I_3^L(t,x_i) - I_3^L(t,x_j)}^p\right] \\\lesssim  &\mathbb E\left[\Parentheses*{\sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor - 1} \mathbb{E} \left[\Parentheses*{ \sum_{\ell}\Parentheses*{q^L_{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor - 1 -k}(x_i,x_\ell) -  q^L_{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor - 1 -k}(x_j,x_\ell)} \eta^L_{k+1}(x_\ell)}^2 \mid\mathcal F_{k}\right] }^{p/2}\right]\\
		&\quad+ \sum_{k = 0}^{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor-1} \mathbb{E}\left[\abs{\sum_{\ell} \Parentheses*{q^L_{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor - 1 -k}(x_i,x_\ell) -  q^L_{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor - 1 -k}(x_j,x_\ell)} \eta^L_{k+1}(x_\ell)}^p\right]\\
		&=:J_1(t,x_i,x_j)+J_2(t,x_i,x_j).
	\end{align*}
	For fixed $x_i,x_j$, define
	\begin{align*}
		\delta_m^{i,j}(x_\ell):= q^L_{m}(x_i,x_\ell) - q^L_m(x_j,x_\ell),
	\end{align*}

	We note that, by independence of $\{\mathcal{R}_{i,g}^L\}_{i,g}$, the sequence $\{\eta^L_{g+1}(x_\ell)\}_{\ell \in \mathbb{Z}}$ is independent, mean zero, conditioned on $\mathcal F_g$ for each $g\in \mathbb{N}$. Hence for all $\ell \neq \ell' \in \mathbb{Z}$,
	\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{E}\left[\eta^L_{k+1}(x_\ell)\eta^L_{k+1}(x_{\ell'})\mid \mathcal F_k\right]
		&=\mathbb{E}\left[\eta^L_{k+1}(x_\ell)\mid \mathcal F_k\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\eta^L_{k+1}(x_{\ell'})\mid \mathcal F_k\right]=0.
	\end{align*}
	Hence, by~\eqref{eq:mtg_increment l2} and Corollary~\ref{cor:gradient l2 bound}, for $m \ge 1$, 
	\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{E}\left[\Parentheses*{\sum_\ell \delta_m^{i,j}(x_\ell) \eta^L_{k+1}(x_\ell)}^2 |\mathcal F_{k}\right] &= \sum_{\ell, \ell'}\mathbb{E}\left[ \delta_m^{i,j}(x_\ell) \delta_m^{i,j}(x_{\ell'}) \eta^L_{k+1}(x_\ell) \eta^L_{k+1}(x_{\ell'})|\mathcal F_{k}\right]\\
		&=\sum_\ell\delta_m^{i,j}(x_\ell)^2 \mathbb{E}\left[ \eta^L_{k+1}(x_\ell)^2 |\mathcal F_{k}\right]\\
		&{\overset{\eqref{eq:mtg_increment l2},\,\eqref{eq:coefficient convergence}}{\lesssim} L^{-1}\sum_\ell \delta_m^{i,j}(x_\ell)^2} \\
		&{\overset{\text{Cor.~\ref{cor:gradient l2 bound}}}{\lesssim} L^{-1} \min\left\{\frac{L\abs{x_i - x_j}}{m}, m^{-1/2} \right\}}.
	\end{align*}
	Note that $\delta_0^{i,j} = \mathbf{1}_{\{x_i\}} - \mathbf{1}_{\{x_j\}}$, and since $L^{-1} \le \abs{x_i -x_j}$ for any $x_i \neq x_j$, we see
	\begin{align*}
		{ L^{-1}\sum_{k = 0}^{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor -1} \sum_{\ell}\delta_{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor -1-k}^{i,j}(x_\ell)^2 \le 2\abs{x_i -x_j}+L^{-1}\sum_{k = 1}^{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor} \sum_\ell \delta_k^{i,j}(x_\ell)^2.}
	\end{align*}
	Consider now for $t\ge L^{-2}$ that
	\begin{align*}
		J_1(t,x_i,x_j) &{\lesssim} \abs{x_i - x_j}^{\frac p2}+\Parentheses*{{ L^{-1}}\Parentheses*{\sum_{k = 1}^{\lfloor \Parentheses*{t\wedge \abs{x_i - x_j}}L^2\rfloor} +\sum^{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor}_{\lfloor \Parentheses*{t\wedge \abs{x_i - x_j}}L^2\rfloor}}\sum_\ell \delta_k^{i,j}(x_\ell)^2}^\frac{p}{2}\\
		&{\lesssim} \abs{x_i - x_j}^{\frac p2}+\Parentheses*{{ L^{-1}}\sum_{k = 1}^{\lfloor \Parentheses*{t\wedge \abs{x_i - x_j}}L^2\rfloor} k^{-1/2}+\sum^{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor}_{\lfloor \Parentheses*{t\wedge \abs{x_i - x_j}}L^2\rfloor} \frac{\abs{x_i - x_j}}{k}}^\frac{p}{2}\\
		&{\lesssim} \abs{x_i - x_j}^{\frac p2}+\Parentheses*{{ L^{-1}} \int_0^{\ \Parentheses*{t\wedge \abs{x_i - x_j}}L^2} s^{-1/2} \mathrm ds + \sum^{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor}_{\lfloor \Parentheses*{t\wedge \abs{x_i - x_j}}L^2\rfloor} \frac{\abs{x_i - x_j}}{k} } ^\frac{p}{2}\\
		&\lesssim  \abs{x_i - x_j}^{\frac p2}+\Parentheses*{\Parentheses*{t\wedge \abs{x_i - x_j}}^\frac{1}{2}+\abs{x_i - x_j} \ln \Parentheses*{\frac{t}{t\wedge |x_i - x_j|}}}^{\frac{p}{2}}\\
		&\lesssim \abs{x_i - x_j}^\frac{p}{4},
	\end{align*}
	where we used the fact that $|x_i - x_j| \le 1$ in the last line.

	For $J_2$ term, by conditional independence and Rosenthal's inequality, we see, for $m\ge1$,
	\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{E}\left[\abs{\sum_\ell \delta_m^{i,j}(x_\ell) \eta^L_{k+1}(x_\ell) } ^p\bigg | \mathcal F_{k}\right]&\lesssim \Parentheses*{\sum_\ell  \delta_m^{i,j}(x_\ell)^2 \mathbb{E} \left[\eta^L_{k+1}(x_\ell)^2\bigg| \mathcal F_{k}\right]}^\frac{p}{2} +\sum_\ell |\delta_m^{i,j}(x_\ell)|^p \mathbb{E}\left[ |\eta^L_{k+1}(x_\ell)|^p \bigg|\mathcal F_{k}\right]\\
		&{\overset{\eqref{eq:mtg_increment l2},\,\eqref{ineq:p_moment bound}}{\lesssim} \Parentheses*{\sum_\ell \delta_m^{i,j}(x_\ell)^2 L^{-1}}^\frac{p}{2} + L^{-1-\kappa_p} \sum_\ell |\delta_m^{i,j}(x_\ell)|^p} \\
		&{\lesssim} \Parentheses*{\min\left\{ \frac{|x_i - x_j|}{m}, L^{-1} m^{-1/2}\right\}}^\frac{p}{2}+ m^{-\frac{p-2}{2}} { L^{-1-\kappa_p}} \sum_\ell \delta_m^{i,j}(x_\ell)^2\\
		&{\lesssim} \frac{|x_i-x_j|^\frac{p}{2}}{m^{p/2}}+ m^{-\frac{p-2}{2}}{ L^{-\kappa_p}}\min\left\{\frac{\abs{x_i-x_j}}{m}, { L^{-1}} m^{-1/2} \right\}\\
		&{\lesssim} m^{-\frac{p}{2}} \Parentheses*{\abs{x_i - x_j}^\frac{p}{2} + { L^{-\kappa_p}}\abs{x_i - x_j}}\\
		&\lesssim m^{-\frac{p}{2}}\Parentheses*{\abs{x_i - x_j}^\frac{p}{2} +\abs{x_i - x_j}^{1+\kappa_p}}\\
		&\lesssim m^{-\frac{p}{2}}\abs{x_i - x_j }^{\frac{p}{2}\wedge \Parentheses*{1+\kappa_p}}
	\end{align*}
	where we used Corollary~\ref{cor:gradient l2 bound} and Corollary~\ref{cor:heat kernel on diagonal bound} in the third line, and the fact that for $x_i \neq x_j$, $|x_i - x_j| \ge L^{-1}$ in the second to the last line, and $\abs{x_i - x_j} \le 1$ in the last line. For $m=0$, then the above equals $\mathbb{E}\Bracket*{\abs{\eta^L_{k+1}(x_i)-\eta^L_{k+1}(x_j)}^p}\lesssim L^{-1-\kappa_p}\le\abs{x_i-x_j}^{1+\kappa_p}$ by~\eqref{ineq:mtg_increment bounds}. Therefore, for any $p >2$,
	\begin{align*}
		J_2(t,x_i,x_j) &\lesssim \abs{x_i - x_j}^{1+\kappa_p}+\abs{x_i - x_j}^{\frac{p}{2}\wedge\Parentheses*{1+\kappa_p}}\sum_{m = 1}^{\infty} m^{-p/2} \lesssim \abs{x_i - x_j}^{\frac{p}{2}\wedge\Parentheses*{1+\kappa_p}}.
	\end{align*}
	Hence, it holds uniformly in $L \in \mathbb{N}$, $\abs{x_i - x_j}\le 1$ and $t \in [0,T]$ that
	\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{E} \Bracket*{\abs{I_3(t,x_i) - I_3(t,x_j)}^p} \lesssim J_1(t,x_i,x_j) +J_2(t,x_i,x_j) \lesssim \abs{x_i - x_j}^{\frac{p}{4}\wedge \Parentheses*{1+\kappa_p}}.
	\end{align*}
	Combined with the moment bounds for $I_2$ term, we see the desired result holds.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- prop-7: body_md -->
\label{prop:temporal_increment_moment_bound}
	For every $T>0$, let $p>8$ and $\kappa_p>3$ be as in~\eqref{ineq:p_moment bound}. Uniformly over all sufficiently large $L$, $x_i\in\mathbb{Z}/L$, and $s,t\in L^{-2}\mathbb{Z}_+$ satisfying $0\le s<t\le T$,
	\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{E}\Bracket*{\abs{I_2^L(t,x_i)-I_2^L(s,x_i)}^p}
		+\mathbb{E}\Bracket*{\abs{I_3^L(t,x_i)-I_3^L(s,x_i)}^p}
		\lesssim_T (t-s)^{\frac{p}{4}\wedge\frac{1+\kappa_p}{2}}.
	\end{align*}
<!-- end prop-7 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
Let us fix an arbitrary $T>0$ and consider first $I_2^L$. Set $h:=(t-s)L^2\ge1$. By the uniform boundedness of $\abs{B_L(\xi^L)}$, we see it holds uniformly in $0\le s,t \le T$ and $L \in \mathbb{N}$ that
	\begin{align*}
		\abs{I_2^L(t,x_i)-I_2^L(s,x_i)}
		&\le L^{-2}\sum_{k=sL^2}^{tL^2-1}\sum_{x_\ell\in\mathbb{Z}/L}
		q_{tL^2-1-k}^L(x_i,x_\ell)
		\abs{B_L(\xi_{k/L^2}^L)(x_\ell)}\\
		&\qquad+L^{-2}\sum_{k=0}^{sL^2-1}\sum_{x_\ell\in\mathbb{Z}/L}
		\abs{q_{tL^2-1-k}^L(x_i,x_\ell)-q_{sL^2-1-k}^L(x_i,x_\ell)}
		\abs{B_L(\xi_{k/L^2}^L)(x_\ell)}\\
		&\lesssim L^{-2}\left(h+
		\sum_{k=0}^{sL^2-1}\sum_{x_\ell\in\mathbb{Z}/L}
		\abs{q_{tL^2-1-k}^L(x_i,x_\ell)-q_{sL^2-1-k}^L(x_i,x_\ell)}\right)\\
		&=L^{-2}\left(h+
		\sum_{n=0}^{sL^2-1}\sum_{x_\ell\in\mathbb{Z}/L}
		\abs{q_{n+h}^L(x_i,x_\ell)-q_n^L(x_i,x_\ell)}\right)\\
		&{\overset{\eqref{ineq:heat kernel temporal l1 difference}}{\lesssim}}
		L^{-2}\left(h+1+
		\sum_{n=1}^{sL^2-1}\min\left\{1,\sqrt{\frac{h}{n}}\right\}\right)\\
		&\lesssim L^{-2}\Parentheses*{h+1+\sqrt{hsL^2}}\\
		&\lesssim (t-s)+\sqrt{s(t-s)}\\
		&\lesssim\sqrt{t-s},
	\end{align*}
	where we used the fact that $h\ge1$ in the sixth line. Hence, it holds uniformly in $L \in \mathbb{N}$, $0 \le s,t \le T$ and $x_i \in \mathbb{Z}/L$ that
	\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{E}\Bracket*{\abs{I_2^L(t,x_i)-I_2^L(s,x_i)}^p}
		\lesssim\abs{t-s}^{p/2}
		\lesssim\abs{t-s}^{\frac p4\wedge\frac{1+\kappa_p}{2}}.
	\end{align*}

	We now prove the estimate for $I_3^L$. Note that
	\begin{align}\label{ineq:temporal difference moment martingale}
		\norm{I_3^L(t,x_i)-I_3^L(s,x_i)}_{L^p_\Omega}^p&\lesssim \norm{\sum_{k = sL^2}^{tL^2 - 1} \sum_\ell q^L_{tL^2-1-k}(x_i,x_\ell) \eta^L_{k+1}(x_\ell)}_{L^p_\Omega}^p\nonumber\\
		&\qquad \qquad + \norm{\sum_{k = 0}^{sL^2 - 1}\sum_\ell \Parentheses*{q^L_{tL^2-1-k}(x_i,x_\ell)- q^L_{sL^2-1-k}(x_i,x_\ell)}\eta^L_{k+1}(x_\ell)}_{L^p_\Omega}^p\nonumber\\
		&:= J_1+J_2.
	\end{align}
	By Rosenthal's inequality, we see it holds uniformly in $L \in \mathbb{N}$, $0 \le s,t\le T$ and $x_i \in \mathbb{Z}/L$ that
	\begin{align*}
		J_1&\lesssim \norm{\sum_{k = sL^2}^{tL^2 - 1} \mathbb{E} \Bracket*{\Parentheses*{\sum_\ell q^L_{tL^2 -1-k}(x_i,x_\ell) \eta^L_{k+1}(x_\ell)}^2\bigg|\mathcal F_{k}}}_{L^{p/2}_\Omega}^{p/2}\\
		&\qquad + \sum_{k = sL^2}^{tL^2 -1} \norm{\sum_\ell q^L_{tL^2 -1-k}(x_i,x_\ell) \eta^L_{k+1}(x_\ell) }_{L^p_\Omega}^p\\
		&:= J_1^1+J_1^2.
	\end{align*}
	By conditional independence and the fact that $h \ge 1$, we see
	\begin{align*}
		J_1^1&=\norm{\sum_{k = sL^2}^{tL^2-1} \sum_\ell q^L_{tL^2-1-k}(x_i,x_\ell)^2\mathbb{E}\Bracket*{\eta^L_{k+1}(x_\ell)^2\bigg |\mathcal F_{k}}}_{L^\frac{p}{2}_\Omega}^\frac{p}{2}\\
		&{\lesssim} \Parentheses*{{ L^{-1}}\sum_{k = sL^2}^{tL^2-1} q^L_{2tL^2-2-2k}(x_i,x_i)}^\frac{p}{2}\\
		&{\overset{\mathrm{Cor.}\,\ref{cor:heat kernel on diagonal bound}}{\lesssim}}\Parentheses*{L^{-1}\Parentheses*{1+\sum_{r=1}^{h-1}r^{-1/2}}}^\frac{p}{2}\\
		&\lesssim\Parentheses*{L^{-1}\sqrt h}^\frac{p}{2}\\
		&=\abs{t-s}^\frac{p}{4}.
	\end{align*}
	Also,
	\begin{align*}
		J_1^2&\lesssim \sum_{k = sL^2}^{tL^2 -1} \mathbb{E}\left[\Parentheses*{\sum_\ell q^L_{tL^2 -1-k}(x_i,x_\ell)^2 \mathbb{E}[\eta^L_{k+1}(x_\ell)^2\mid\mathcal F_k]} ^\frac{p}{2}\right]+\sum_{k = sL^2}^{tL^2 -1} \sum_\ell  q^L_{tL^2 -1-k}(x_i,x_\ell)^p \norm{\eta^L_{k+1}(x_\ell)}_{L^p_\Omega}^p.
	\end{align*}
	First term on the right hand side is bounded by
	\begin{align*}
		\sum_{k = sL^2}^{tL^2 -1} \Parentheses*{\sum_\ell q^L_{tL^2 -1-k}(x_i,x_\ell)^2{ L^{-1}} }^\frac{p}{2}&{\overset{\mathrm{Cor.}\,\ref{cor:heat kernel on diagonal bound}}{\lesssim}} { L^{-\frac{p}{2}}}\sum_{k = sL^2}^{tL^2 -2} \Parentheses*{tL^2-1-k}^{-p/4} + { L^{-p/2}}\\
		&{\lesssim} L^{-\frac{p}{2}}\Parentheses*{1+ \sum_{k = 1}^\infty k^{-\frac{p}{4}}}\\
		&\lesssim L^{-\frac{p}{2}} \le (t-s)^\frac{p}{4},
	\end{align*}
	where we used the fact that  $t-s\ge L^{-2}$ and $p >8$ in the last line. Consider now the second term on the right hand side for $J_1^2$,
	\begin{align*}
		\sum_{k = sL^2}^{tL^2 -1} \sum_\ell  q^L_{tL^2 -1-k}(x_i,x_\ell)^p \norm{\eta^L_{k+1}(x_\ell)}_{L^p_\Omega}^p&{\overset{\eqref{ineq:p_moment bound}}{\lesssim}}{ L^{-1-\kappa_p}\sum_{k=sL^2}^{tL^2-1}\sum_\ell q^L_{tL^2-1-k}(x_i,x_\ell)^p}\\
		&\le L^{-1-\kappa_p} \sum_{k = sL^2}^{tL^2-1} \Parentheses*{\sup_\ell q^L_{tL^2-1-k}(x_i,x_\ell)}^{p-1}\sum_\ell q^L_{tL^2-1-k}(x_i,x_\ell)\\
		&{\overset{\mathrm{Cor.}\,\ref{cor:heat kernel on diagonal bound}}{\lesssim}} { L^{-1-\kappa_p}}\Parentheses*{1+\sum_{k=1}^{(t-s)L^2-1}k^{-\frac{p-1}{2}}}\\
		&{\lesssim} { L^{-1-\kappa_p}}\\
		&{\le} {(t-s)^{\frac{1+\kappa_p}{2}}},
	\end{align*}
	where we used the fact that $p>8$ in the fourth inequality and  $t-s\ge L^{-2}$ in the last inequality. Combining the previous estimates to see
	\begin{align}\label{ineq:temporal J1}
		J_1 \lesssim J_1^1+J_1^2 \lesssim \abs{t-s}^{\frac{p}{4}\wedge \frac{1+\kappa_p}{2}}.
	\end{align}

	For $J_2$, let us denote for $0\le s,t \le T$, $i \in \mathbb{Z}$, $k \in \mathbb{N}$ and $x_\ell \in \mathbb{Z}/L$ that
	\begin{align*}
		\delta_k^{s,t;i}(x_\ell)
		:=\abs{q^L_{tL^2-k-1}(x_i,x_\ell)-q^L_{sL^2-k-1}(x_i,x_\ell)}.
	\end{align*}
	Similar to the $J_1$ term, we may use Rosenthal's inequality in $k$, followed by conditional Rosenthal's inequality in $x_\ell$, to see that
	\begin{align*}
		J_2 \lesssim J_2^1+J_2^2+J_2^3,
	\end{align*}
	where
	\begin{align*}
		J_2^1&:=\mathbb{E}\Bracket*{\Parentheses*{\sum_{k=0}^{sL^2-1}\sum_\ell
		\delta_k^{s,t;i}(x_\ell)^2
		\mathbb{E}\Bracket*{\eta_{k+1}^L(x_\ell)^2\mid\mathcal F_k}}^{p/2}},\\
		J_2^2&:=\sum_{k=0}^{sL^2-1}\mathbb{E}\Bracket*{\Parentheses*{\sum_\ell
		\delta_k^{s,t;i}(x_\ell)^2
		\mathbb{E}\Bracket*{\eta_{k+1}^L(x_\ell)^2\mid\mathcal F_k}}^{p/2}},\\
		J_2^3&:=\sum_{k=0}^{sL^2-1}\sum_\ell
		\mathbb{E}\Bracket*{\delta_k^{s,t;i}(x_\ell)^p
		\abs{\eta_{k+1}^L(x_\ell)}^p}.
	\end{align*}

	Recall $h=(t-s)L^2$. By Corollary~\ref{cor:heat kernel on diagonal bound} and Lemma~\ref{lem: heat kernel temporal l2 difference bound}, it holds uniformly in $h,n \in \mathbb{N}$ that
	\begin{align}\label{ineq:heat kernel square time difference}
		\sum_\ell\abs{q^L_{n+h}(x_i,x_\ell)-q^L_n(x_i,x_\ell)}^2
		\lesssim\min\left\{n^{-1/2},hn^{-3/2}\right\},
	\end{align}
	Hence, it holds uniformly in $L \in \mathbb{N}$, $0\le s,t\le T$ and  $x_i \in \mathbb{Z}/L$ that
	\begin{align*}
		J_2^1 &{\overset{\eqref{eq:coefficient convergence}}{\lesssim}} \Parentheses*{L^{-1}\sum_{k = 0}^{sL^2 - 1} \sum_\ell \delta_k^{s,t;i}(x_\ell)^2}^\frac{p}{2}\\
		&{\overset{\eqref{ineq:heat kernel square time difference}}{\lesssim}}\Parentheses*{L^{-1}\Parentheses*{1+\sum_{n=1}^{h}n^{-1/2}
		+h\sum_{n=h+1}^{\infty}n^{-3/2}}}^{p/2}\\
		&\lesssim\Parentheses*{L^{-1}\Parentheses*{1+\sqrt h}}^{p/2}\\
		&\lesssim\Parentheses*{L^{-1}\sqrt h}^{p/2}=(t-s)^{p/4}.
	\end{align*}
	where we used the fact that $h \ge 1$ in the last inequality. Similarly, since $p>8$,
	\begin{align*}
		J_2^2
		&\lesssim L^{-p/2}\left(1+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}
		\min\left\{n^{-1/2},hn^{-3/2}\right\}^{p/2}\right)\le L^{-p/2}\left(1+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}n^{-p/4}\right)
		\lesssim (L^{-2})^{p/4}
		\le(t-s)^{p/4},
	\end{align*}
	where the last inequality uses $t-s\ge L^{-2}$.

	By Corollary~\ref{cor:heat kernel on diagonal bound}, it holds uniformly in  $L,n,h \in \mathbb{N}$ and $x_i \in \mathbb{Z}/L$ that
	\begin{align*}
		\sum_\ell\abs{q^L_{n+h}(x_i,x_\ell)-q^L_n(x_i,x_\ell)}^p
		&\le\sup_\ell\Parentheses*{q^L_{n+h}(x_i,x_\ell)+q^L_n(x_i,x_\ell)}^{p-1}\sum_\ell\Parentheses*{q^L_{n+h}(x_i,x_\ell)+q^L_n(x_i,x_\ell)}\\
		&\lesssim n^{-(p-1)/2}.
	\end{align*}
	Thus, using the fact that $\abs{t-s} \ge L^{-2}$ when $t \neq s$, we see it holds uniformly in $L \in \mathbb{N}$, $0\le s,t \le T$ and $x_i \in \mathbb{Z}/L$ that
	\begin{align*}
		J_2^3
		&{\overset{\eqref{ineq:mtg_increment bounds}}{\lesssim}}L^{-1-\kappa_p} \sum_{k = 0}^{sL^2 - 1} \sum_\ell\abs{\delta^{s,t;i}_k(x_\ell)}^p\\
		&\lesssim L^{-1-\kappa_p} \Parentheses*{1+\sum_{k = 1}^\infty k^{-\frac{p-1}{2}}}\\
		&\lesssim L^{-1-\kappa_p}
		\le(t-s)^{(1+\kappa_p)/2}.
	\end{align*}
	Collecting the above estimates to see it holds uniformly in $L \in \mathbb{N}$, $0\le t,s \le T$  and $x_i \in \mathbb{Z}/L$ that
	\begin{align*}
		J_2 \lesssim \abs{t-s}^{\frac{p}{4}\wedge\frac{1+\kappa_p}{2}}.
	\end{align*}
	Inserting this and~\eqref{ineq:temporal J1} into~\eqref{ineq:temporal difference moment martingale} to see
	\begin{align*}
		\mathbb{E}\Bracket*{\abs{I_3^L(t,x_i)-I_3^L(s,x_i)}^p}
		&\lesssim\abs{t-s}^{\frac p4\wedge\frac{1+\kappa_p}{2}},
	\end{align*}
	which is the desired result.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{lem:initial convergence}
	Suppose $u_0 \in \mathcal C_b(\mathbb{R})$ and  $u_L:\mathbb{Z}/L\to\mathbb{R}$ for each $L \in \mathbb{N}$ is bounded and satisfies, for every $\lambda>0$,
	\begin{align}\label{ineq:initial discretized convergence weighted}
		\norm{u_L-\pi_L^\text{disc}u_0}_{(\lambda),L}\longrightarrow 0,\qquad \text{ as }L \to \infty.
	\end{align}
	For $t\ge0$, set $A_L(t):=\pi_L^\text{int}P^L_{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor}u_L$. Then for every $T,\lambda>0$,
	\begin{align}\label{ineq:initial semigroup convergence}
		\lim_{L \to \infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\norm{A_L(t)-P_tu_0}_{(\lambda)}=0.
	\end{align}
	Let $\widehat A_L$ be the temporal interpolation of $A_L$, then for each $T,\lambda >0$, it holds uniformly in $L \in \mathbb{N}$ that
	\begin{align}\label{ineq:initial temporal interpolation gap}
		\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\norm{A_L(t)-\widehat A_L(t)}_{(\lambda)}
		\lesssim
		\norm{u_L-\pi_L^\text{disc}u_0}_{(\lambda),L}
		+\omega_{u_0,\lambda}(1/L),
	\end{align}
	which converges to zero as $L\to\infty$.
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
Let $T,\lambda >0$ be arbitrary but fixed. We will first prove~\eqref{ineq:initial semigroup convergence}.  For each $L \in \mathbb{N}$, set $r_L:=u_L-\pi_L^\text{disc}u_0$. We first note that, for any $f:\mathbb{Z}/L\to\mathbb{R}$ with $\norm{f}_{(\lambda),L}<\infty$,
	\begin{align}\label{ineq:continuous and discrete weighted norm}
		\norm{\pi_L^\text{int}f}_{(\lambda)}\le e^{\lambda/L}\norm{f}_{(\lambda),L}.
	\end{align}
	Indeed, if $x\in[x_i,x_{i+1}]$, then $\pi_L^\text{int}f(x)$ is a convex combination of $f(x_i)$ and $f(x_{i+1})$, and $\max\{|x_i|-|x|,|x_{i+1}|-|x|\}\le L^{-1}$. Hence, by Lemma~\ref{lem:weighted discrete semigroup stability},~\eqref{ineq:continuous and discrete weighted norm} and~\eqref{ineq:initial discretized convergence weighted},
	\begin{align*}
		\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\norm{\pi_L^\text{int}P^L_{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor}r_L}_{(\lambda)}
		&\le 2e^{\lambda/L}\exp\left\{\frac{\alpha T\lambda^2}{2}\cosh\lambda\right\}\norm{r_L}_{(\lambda),L}\longrightarrow0, \qquad \text{ as  }L \to \infty,
	\end{align*}
	Therefore, by the triangle inequality, it suffices to prove
	\begin{align*}
		\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\|\pi_L^\text{int}P^L_{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor}\pi_L^\text{disc}u_0-P_tu_0\right\|_{(\lambda)}\longrightarrow0,\qquad \text{as }L \to \infty.
	\end{align*}

	Suppose first that $u_0\in\mathcal C_0(\mathbb{R})$. For every $\varphi\in\mathcal C_c^\infty(\mathbb{R})$ and $x_i \in \mathbb{Z}/L$,
	\begin{align*}
		L^2(P_1^L-I)\pi_L^\text{disc}\varphi(x_i)
		=
		\frac{\alpha L^2}{2}
		\bigl(
		\varphi(x_i+L^{-1})-2\varphi(x_i)
		+\varphi(x_i-L^{-1})
		\bigr).
	\end{align*}
	Taylor's theorem therefore gives
	\begin{align*}
		\sup_{x_i\in\mathbb{Z}/L}
		\left|
		L^2(P_1^L-I)\pi_L^\text{disc}\varphi(x_i)
		-\frac{\alpha}{2}\varphi''(x_i)
		\right|
		&\longrightarrow0 \qquad \text{ as }L \to \infty.
	\end{align*}
	Since $\mathcal C_c^\infty(\mathbb{R})$ is a core for the generator $\frac{\alpha}{2}\Delta$ of the heat semigroup on $\mathcal C_0(\mathbb{R})$, we may apply \cite{ethier2009markov}*{Chapter 1, Theorem 6.5} with the contractions $P_1^L$ on the supremum-norm spaces of lattice functions that vanish at infinity, time steps $L^{-2}$, and comparison maps $\pi_L^\text{disc}$. It follows that
	\begin{align*}
		J_{1,L}
		:=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{x_i\in\mathbb{Z}/L}
		\abs{P^L_{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor}\pi_L^\text{disc}u_0(x_i)-P_tu_0(x_i)}
		\longrightarrow0,\qquad \text{ as } L \to \infty.
	\end{align*}
	Because $\pi_L^\text{int}$ is a contraction in the supremum norm, we see that for all $L \in \mathbb{N}$,
	\begin{align*}
		\sup_{t\in[0,T]}
		\left\|
		\pi_L^\text{int}P^L_{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor}\pi_L^\text{disc}u_0
		-\pi_L^\text{int}\pi_L^\text{disc}P_tu_0
		\right\|_{\mathcal C_0(\mathbb{R})}
		\le J_{1,L}.
	\end{align*}
	Moreover, for $t>0$ and $x,y\in\mathbb{R}$,
	\begin{align*}
		\abs{P_tu_0(x)-P_tu_0(y)}
		&\le \int_\mathbb{R} G_t(z)\abs{u_0(x-z)-u_0(y-z)}\,\mathrm dz
		\le \omega_{u_0}(\abs{x-y}),
	\end{align*}
	and the same bound holds at $t=0$ because $P_0u_0=u_0$. Hence, it holds for all $L \in \mathbb{N}$   that
	\begin{align*}
		\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\norm{P_tu_0-\pi_L^\text{int}\pi_L^\text{disc}P_tu_0}_{\mathcal C_0(\mathbb{R})}
		\le \omega_{u_0}(1/L).
	\end{align*}
	Since every $u_0\in\mathcal C_0(\mathbb{R})$ is uniformly continuous, $\omega_{u_0}(1/L)\to0$. Together with $J_{1,L}\to0$, this proves the desired convergence for $u_0\in\mathcal C_0(\mathbb{R})$.

	Now suppose $u_0 \in \mathcal C_b(\mathbb{R})$. For a bounded continuous function $v$, set
	\begin{align*}
		D_L(v):=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\norm{\pi_L^\text{int}P^L_{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor}\pi_L^\text{disc}v-P_tv}_{(\lambda)}.
	\end{align*}
	For each $M\ge1$, choose a continuous function $\chi_M:\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]$ such that $\chi_M=1$ on $[-M,M]$ and $\chi_M=0$ outside $[-M-1,M+1]$, and set $v_M:=(1-\chi_M)u_0$. Then
	\begin{align*}
		D_L(u_0)\le D_L(\chi_Mu_0)+D_L(v_M),
	\end{align*}
	where $D_L(\chi_Mu_0)\to0$ for each fixed $M$ by the preceding $\mathcal C_0(\mathbb{R})$ case.

	We next control $D_L(v_M)$ uniformly in $L$. Fix $M_0>0$ and take $M>M_0+1$, with $R:=M-M_0-1$. Since $P_k^L$, $P_t$, and $\pi_L^\text{int}$ are contractions in the supremum norm,
	\begin{align*}
		\sup_{\substack{|x|>M_0\\t\in[0,T]}}
		e^{-\lambda|x|}\abs{\pi_L^\text{int}P^L_{\lfloor tL^2\rfloor}\pi_L^\text{disc}v_M(x)-P_tv_M(x)}
		\le 2\norm{u_0}_{\mathcal C_b(\mathbb{R})}e^{-\lambda M_0}.
	\end{align*}
	Since $v_M$ vanishes on $[-M,M]$, translation invariance of $q_k^L$ gives, for all $x_j \in \mathbb{Z}/L$ with $\abs{x_j} \le M_0+1$, it holds that
	\begin{align*}
		\abs{P_k^L\pi_L^\text{disc}v_M(x_j)}
		&\le \norm{u_0}_{\mathcal C_b(\mathbb{R})}
		\sum_{|x_\ell|>M}q_k^L(0,x_\ell-x_j)\\
		&\le \norm{u_0}_{\mathcal C_b(\mathbb{R})}
		\sum_{|z_\ell|>R}q_k^L(0,z_\ell),
	\end{align*}
	In the last line we wrote $z_\ell:=x_\ell-x_j$ and used the fact that $|z_\ell|\ge |x_\ell|-|x_j|>M-M_0-1=R$. Since for all $\abs{x} \le M_0$, there exists $x_i \in \mathbb{Z}/L$ with $\abs{x_i} \le M_0+1$ and $x \in [x_{i-1},x_{i}]$, it follows that
	\begin{align*}
		\sup_{\substack{|x|\le M_0\\k\le TL^2}}
		\abs{\pi_L^\text{int}P^L_k\pi_L^\text{disc}v_M(x)}
		&\le \norm{u_0}_{\mathcal C_b(\mathbb{R})}
		\sup_{k\le TL^2}\sum_{|z_\ell|>R}q_k^L(0,z_\ell).
	\end{align*}
	Similarly, for $|x|\le M_0$ and $t>0$,
	\begin{align*}
		\abs{P_tv_M(x)}
		&\le \norm{u_0}_{\mathcal C_b(\mathbb{R})}
		\int_{|y|>M}G_t(x-y)\,\mathrm dy\\
		&\le \norm{u_0}_{\mathcal C_b(\mathbb{R})}
		\int_{|z|>R}G_t(z)\,\mathrm dz,
	\end{align*}
	At $t=0$, both $\pi_L^\text{int}\pi_L^\text{disc}v_M(x)$ and $P_0v_M(x)=v_M(x)$ vanish for $|x|\le M_0$. Therefore,
	\begin{align*}
		\sup_{\substack{|x|\le M_0\\t\in(0,T]}}
		\abs{P_tv_M(x)}
		&\le \norm{u_0}_{\mathcal C_b(\mathbb{R})}
		\sup_{t\in(0,T]}\int_{|z|>R}G_t(z)\,\mathrm dz.
	\end{align*}
	Under $q_k^L(0,\cdot)$, the coordinate variable has the same law as $S_k/L$ and therefore has variance $\alpha k/L^2$; likewise, $G_t$ has variance $\alpha t$. Hence Chebyshev's inequality yields
	\begin{align*}
		\sup_{k\le TL^2}\sum_{|z_\ell|>R}q_k^L(0,z_\ell)
		+\sup_{t\in(0,T]}\int_{\abs{z}>R}G_t(z)\,\mathrm dz
		\le \frac{2\alpha T}{R^2}.
	\end{align*}
	Consequently, for every $L\in\mathbb{N}$,
	\begin{align*}
		D_L(v_M)
		\le 2\norm{u_0}_{\mathcal C_b(\mathbb{R})}e^{-\lambda M_0}
		+\frac{2\alpha T}{R^2}\norm{u_0}_{\mathcal C_b(\mathbb{R})}.
	\end{align*}
